\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}},\;{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}=({\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\mathbf{D}},

by saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
.

Since
{\mathbf{D}}
is potentially semistable, it is de Rham. Hence we have

D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})\cong(D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}})\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{{\rm Gal}(L^{\prime}/L)},\;

which is a free
L\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
-module of rank
n
. The module
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
is equipped with a natural Hodge filtration. We assume that
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
has distinct Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Denote by
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Now by Berger’s equivalence of categories, we see that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
corresponds to the filtered Delingen-Fontaine module
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{DF}}({\mathbf{D}})
equipped with the induced filtration from the Hodge filtration on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}})
. We say that
\mathcal{F}
is
non-critical
if the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
are given by

\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}.\;

This implies that the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
are

\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i-1)r+1},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i-1)r+2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}.\;

In this case, using Berger’s equivalence of categories
[
4
, Theorem A]
and comparing the weight (or see
[
12
, (2.4)]
), we have an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{i}:{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle\;{\Delta_{\pi}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{i-k})){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}})

(14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}}),

for
i=1,\cdots,k
. Note that this injection (
14
) is equivalent to the existence of an isomorphism of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]
:

{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}[1/t]\cong{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}})[1/t]
or an injection (by comparing Hodge-Tate weights)

{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\hookrightarrow{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{(i-1)r+1}}).\;

This implies that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a non-critical
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
. The parameters of
\mathcal{F}
in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
or
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
are given as follows. Recall that
\pi\cong\pi_{0}\otimes{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi})
for some
\alpha_{\pi}\in E^{\times}
.

Definition 2.2
.

(Non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration)
Keep the above assumption. We say that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
with parameter

\displaystyle({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}},\;\hskip 110.0pt

(15)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{x}}_{\pi}=({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i}\cong x_{\pi})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}}:=(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}={\rm unr}(q_{L}^{i-k}){z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}}})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;

or with parameter

\displaystyle(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\;\hskip 100.0pt

(16)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;\pi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}},i}}\cong\pi_{0}{\otimes_{E}}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi}q_{L}^{i-k}z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}}(\varpi_{L})),\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}}=(\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}={z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}})_{1\leq i\leq k}.

Remark 2.3
.

For convenience, we may use these two kinds of parameters depending on the situation. By
[
12
, Lemma 4.1.9]
, the parameters of
\mathcal{F}
in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
or
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times

{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
are in general not unique. All the parameters of
\mathcal{F}
in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
are of form
(\underline{x}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
such that, for
1\leq i\leq k
, we have
\mathbf{WD}_{x_{i}}\cong\mathbf{WD}_{{\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i}}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{i})
and
\delta^{\prime}_{i}=\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{i}^{-1})\eta_{i}
for some
\alpha_{i}\in\overline{E}^{\times}
and
\eta_{i}\in\mu_{\Omega_{r,i}}=\mu_{\Omega_{r}}
. All the parameters of
\mathcal{F}
in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
are of the form
(\underline{x}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime})
such that, for
1\leq i\leq k
, we have
\mathbf{WD}_{x_{i}}\cong\mathbf{WD}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i}}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\eta_{i}(\varpi_{L}))
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{i}={z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}}}\eta_{i}|_{\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}}
for some
\eta_{i}\in\mu_{\Omega_{r}}
.

Definition 2.4
.

(Non-critical special
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration)
We call an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
is non-critical special with parameter

({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}},\;

(resp., with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
) if
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
(see Definition
2.2
) with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
(resp., with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
), and the subquotient
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}
is non-split for all
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
.

Throughout section
2
, we fix such a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
(in Definition
2.2
or Definition
2.4
). For
1\leq i\leq j\leq k
, we put
{\mathbf{D}}^{j}_{i}:={\rm Fil}_{j}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}/{\rm Fil}_{i-1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
. We also use
\mathcal{F}
to denote the induced non-critical special
\Omega_{[i,j]}
-filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}^{j}_{i}
, where
\Omega_{[i,j]}:=\prod_{l=i}^{j}\Omega_{r,l}
. Clearly, a parameter of this non-critical special
\Omega_{[i,j]}
-filtration is
(({\mathbf{x}}_{0,s})_{i\leq s\leq j},(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s})_{i\leq s\leq j})
.

2.3
Deformation of type
\Omega_{[1,k]}

In this section, we study certain paraboline deformations of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules which admit an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration, under the framework of
[
12
, Section 4.1]
.

We first recall the contents of
[
12
, Section 4.1.1]
. Let
\Delta
be an irreducible
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
, de Rham of constant Hodge-Tate weight
0
. Let
D
be a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
such that there exists a continuous character
\delta:L^{\times}\rightarrow E^{\times}
such that we have an injection
D\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}^{-1})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Delta}
of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
.

In
[
12
, Section 4.1.1]
, the authors consider the following functor:

F_{D}^{0}:{\rm Art}(E):=\{\text{Artinian local $E$-algebra with residue field $E$}\}\longrightarrow\{\text{sets}\}

sends
A
to the set of isomorphism classes
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\delta_{A})\}/\sim
, where

(1)

D_{A}
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
with
\pi_{A}:D_{A}\otimes_{A}E\cong D
;

(2)

{\delta}_{A}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
is a continuous character such that
{\delta}_{A}\equiv{\delta}\mod\mathfrak{m}_{A}
;

(3)

there exists an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
:
D_{A}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Delta}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A})
.

Remark 2.5
.

We remark some easy facts about
F_{D}^{0}
. We consider such deformations for
two reasons.

•

We consider
D_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
of
D
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
. The condition that
D_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
is a subobject of the form
{\Delta}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}})
is more flexible than that
D_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
itself is of that form. In fact, by

[
12
, Proposition 4.1.4]
) or Lemma
2.9
, we see that the following map

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D,D)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D,{\Delta}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\delta}))

is not injective, and its kernel has dimension
d_{L}\frac{r(r-1)}{2}
.

•

We can view
F_{D}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
as a "determinant" part of
F_{D}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
, which is easier to control.

Recall that in Definition
2.2
we have fixed a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
which admits a non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
.

Denote by
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
the deformation functor

F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}:{\rm Art}(E):=\{\text{Artinian local $E$-algebra with residue field $E$}\}\longrightarrow\{\text{sets}\}

sends
A
to the set of isomorphism classes
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{F}_{A},\delta_{A})\}/\sim
, where

(1)

D_{A}
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
n
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
with
\pi_{A}:D_{A}\otimes_{A}E\cong{\mathbf{D}}
;

(2)

\mathcal{F}_{A}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}D_{A}
is an increasing filtration of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
on
D_{A}
, such that
{\rm Fil}_{i}D_{A}
are direct summand of
D_{A}
as
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
-modules, and
\pi_{A}({\rm Fil}_{i}D_{A})\cong{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
;

(3)

{\delta}_{A}=({\delta}_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}
where
{\delta}_{A,i}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
is a continuous character such that
{\delta}_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}(\mod\mathfrak{m}_{A})
;

(4)

there exists an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
:

{\rm gr}_{i}D_{A}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\Delta_{\pi}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A,i}).

(17)

Note that the injection (
17
) is equivalent to the existence of an isomorphism of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[1/t]
:

{\rm gr}_{i}D_{A}[1/t]\cong{\Delta_{\pi}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A,i})[1/t]
. This deformation functor encodes certain paraboline deformations of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules of
{\mathbf{D}}
which admit an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration.

Define by
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
the deformation functor which sends
A\in{\rm Art}(E)
to the isomorphism classes
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}(A):=\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{F}_{A})\}/\sim
. Moreover, the map sending the data
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{F}_{A},\delta_{A})\}
(resp.,

\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{F}_{A})\}
) to
\{({\rm gr}_{i}D_{A},\pi_{A}|_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{A}},{\delta}_{A,i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\}
(resp.,
\{({\rm gr}_{i}D_{A},\pi_{A}|_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{A}}\}
) induces a natural morphism

\Upsilon^{0}:F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}\longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}}^{0}\;\;\;(\text{resp.,\;}\Upsilon:F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}\longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}})

(18)

By definition, we have a natural morphism
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}\rightarrow F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
. We see that

F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}=\mathscr{F}_{D,\mathcal{F}}\times_{\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}D}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}D}^{0}.

We have the following
E
-linear maps

\displaystyle\kappa:F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\xrightarrow{\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{k}

\displaystyle F_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

(19)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\omega^{0}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\kappa_{L}}\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E),

where the first map is obtained by evaluating the morphism
\Upsilon^{0}
on
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
-points, and the second map is given by

((D_{A,i},\pi_{A,i},\delta_{A,i}))_{1\leq i\leq k}\mapsto((\delta_{i}^{-1}-1)/\epsilon)_{1\leq i\leq k}
. The last map
\kappa_{L}
sends
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\cdots,\psi_{k})
to
(\psi_{i}-\psi_{i+1})_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}
. By definition, the composition of
\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
and
\omega^{0}
is given by

({\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},\pi_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},\mathcal{F}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},\delta_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}})\mapsto(({\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i}\delta_{i}^{-1}-1)/\epsilon)_{1\leq i\leq k}.

In Section
2.5
, we see that the functor
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
is pro-representable. We next compute the
E
-dimension of tangent space
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
by using Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula.

Remark 2.6
.

The deformation functor
F_{D,\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}^{0}
in
[
12
, Section 4.1.2]
has generic parameters (
[
12
, (4.13)]
). This functor is formally smooth, which has a simpler structure than our
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
. Proposition 4.1.17 in
[
12
]
also computes its dimension. Note that the parameters of our non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
are non-generic, thus the discussions in
[
12
, Section 4.1.2]
are not valid for our case. We study our deformation functor
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
by working a bit more carefully. One should note that, as opposed to generic case, there are obstructions to paraboline deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
. However, these obstructions to paraboline deformations are characterized by the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants (see Section
2.4
and Section
2.5
below). This result overcomes the above difficulty.

2.4
Parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants

Suppose that
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
(see Definition
2.2
) with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
. In this section, we will attach to
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})
.

For
1\leq i\leq k
, we write

{\mathbf{D}}_{i}:={\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}},\;\Delta_{\pi,i}:={\Delta_{\pi}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i})

for simplicity. We first explain the construction of
pairing (
2
)

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})\;\;\times

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}(L^{\times},E)\big(\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\big)

(20)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cup}E\big(\cong{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}^{-1}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}))\big)

for each
1\leq i\leq k-1
. By
[
38
]
, the cup product induces a pairing

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})\;\;\times\;\;{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})\xrightarrow{\cup}{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i}).}

(21)

We will show below that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})=\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})=1+r^{2}
and

{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})\cong E
. If
r>1
, this pairing is not perfect. Roughly speaking, our pairing (
20
) is obtained by considering the cup product of the first term
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})
and a certain subspace
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
of the second term
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})
in (
21
). We will prove that (
20
) is non-degenerate (but may not be perfect). Then the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants are defined in terms of this pairing. We need the following preliminaries.

The following lemma is a direct consequence of
[
12
, Lemma 4.1.12]
.

Lemma 2.7
.

We have

(a)

For any
j\neq i-1
, we have

{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{j})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{j},{\mathbf{D}}_{i}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}}))=0.

(b)

For any
i\neq j
, we have
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{j})=0
.

Beweis.

Note that for the pair
(i,j)
such that
j\neq i-1
,
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i})
and
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,j},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},j})
satisfy the generic condition of
[
12
, (4.13)]
. Then
(a)
is a direct consequence of
[
12
, Lemma 4.1.12]
. When
i\neq j
, the proof of
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{j})=0
in
[
12
, Lemma 4.1.12]
is also appropriate for our case (but is false for
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i-1})
).
∎

By (
14
), the injections

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{i}:{\mathbf{D}}_{i}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{\pi,i},\;\mathbf{I}_{i+1}:{\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{\pi,i+1}.\;

induces the following natural morphisms:

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbf{I}_{i}^{2,j}}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},\Delta_{\pi,i})

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\mathbf{I}_{i+1}^{1,j}}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1},\Delta_{\pi,i}),

(22)

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1},\Delta_{\pi,i})

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\mathbf{I}_{i}^{2,j}}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbf{I}_{i+1}^{1,j}}

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{j}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1},{\mathbf{D}}_{i}).

for
j=0,1,2
.

Lemma 2.8
.

For
l\in\{1,2\}
and
j\in\{0,1,2\}
, the morphisms
\mathbf{I}_{i}^{l,j}
and
\mathbf{I}_{i+1}^{l,j}
in (
22
) induce isomorphisms.

Beweis.

Let
D_{0}\hookrightarrow D_{1}
be an injection of
(\phi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
with the same rank. After identifying the cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules and the Galois cohomology of
E
-
B
-pairs (for example
[
39
, Section 3]
), we deduce from the morphism
D_{0}\hookrightarrow D_{1}
a long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{0})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0})

(23)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{0})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{0})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0}).

By
[
38
, Theorem 4.7]
, we have
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0})=0
and

\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0})=\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0}).

(24)

For a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
D^{\prime}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
, denoted by
{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D^{\prime})
the
{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
associated to
D^{\prime}
. For
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}:={B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}\otimes_{L,\tau}E
. By
[
14
, Lemma 5.1.1]
, we have

{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D_{1})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D_{0})),

(25)

Applying the above discussion to the pairs

\displaystyle(D_{0},D_{1})=

\displaystyle({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i}),\;

(26)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i}),

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i},\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i}),\;

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i},{\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i}).\;

Note that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i})

(27)

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{s=1}^{r}\oplus_{l=1}^{r}t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir+s}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}/t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i-1)r+l}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir+s}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E},

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{s=1}^{r}\oplus_{l=1}^{r}t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir+s}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}/t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i+1)r}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E},

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\Delta_{\pi,i})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{s=1}^{r}\oplus_{l=1}^{r}t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i+1)r}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}/t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i-1)r+l}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i+1)r}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E},

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}({\mathbf{D}}_{i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(\Delta_{\pi,i+1}^{\vee}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}{\mathbf{D}}_{i})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{s=1}^{r}\oplus_{l=1}^{r}t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i-1)r+l}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,ir+s}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}/t^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i-1)r+l}-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(i+1)r}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}.

All the choices of
(D_{0},D_{1})
satisfy
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D_{1})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D_{0}))=0
. Then we have

{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(D_{1}/D_{0})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D_{1})/{W^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}(D_{0}))=0.\;

Now our lemma is a direct consequence of the long exact sequence (
23
).
∎

.

The morphism

Beweis.

By using Tate duality, it suffices to prove that

is an isomorphism, i.e.,
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}^{0}(\Delta_{\pi}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}\cdot\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}))=0
. Indeed, the unique (up to scalar) nonzero element in

corresponds to an injection

. Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}\cdot\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1})^{+}_{{\rm dR}})/{\mathbf{W}}^{0,+}_{{\rm dR}}\cong\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\big(t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{(i+1)r,\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{ir,\tau}}{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}/{B^{+}_{\mathrm{dR}}}_{,\tau,E}\big)^{\oplus(r^{2}-1)},

we deduce

. By an easy dévissage argument, we see that

Remark 2.14
.

By definition, we have
\langle\psi,[{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}]\rangle_{{\mathbf{c}}_{i}}=\langle\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime}_{i}(\psi),({\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}})^{i+1}_{i}\rangle_{c_{4}}
(see (

)).

Lemma 2.15
.

The following are equivalent:

(a)

For all
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
,
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}
is potentially semistable and noncrystalline,

(b)

For all
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
,
{(\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{z})}^{i+1}_{i}
is semistable noncrystalline,

(c)

For all
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
,
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)\nsubseteq\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}
,

(d)

We have
N^{k-1}\neq 0
on
{\mathbf{DF}}({\mathbf{D}})
, i.e,
{\mathbf{DF}}({\mathbf{D}})
is absolutely indecomposable.

Beweis.

For any
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
, the cup product induces a perfect paring (see
[
25
, Lemma 1.13]
)

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1}))\times{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\xrightarrow{\cup}{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))\cong E.

Then
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma),e}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1}))^{\bot}:=\{\psi\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\;|\;\langle\psi,{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma),e}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1}))\rangle=0\}
is isomorphic to
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)
(see
[
25
, Proposition 1.9, Lemma 1.15]
). Thus we see that

{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)\subseteq\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}

if and only if
{(\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{z})}^{i+1}_{i}
is crystalline. Therefore
(b)
and
(c)
are equivalent.

We next show that
(a)
is equivalent to
(b)
. Since
{\mathbf{D}}_{i}^{i+1}[\frac{1}{t}]=(\Delta_{\mathbf{D}})^{i+1}_{i}[\frac{1}{t}]
, Part (a) is equivalent to prove that
(\Delta_{\mathbf{D}})^{i+1}_{i}={(\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{z})}^{i+1}_{i}\oplus{(\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{0})}^{i+1}_{i}
is potentially semistable noncrystalline. It suffices to show that
{(\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{0})}^{i+1}_{i}
is always potentially crystalline. Let
L^{\prime}
be a sufficiently large extension of
L
such that the restriction of
{\rm End}^{0}({\Delta_{\pi}}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1})
to
L^{\prime}
is crystalline. Recall that
{\mathbf{W}}^{0}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1})
) is the
E
-
B
-pair associated to
{\rm End}^{0}({\Delta_{\pi}})
(resp.,
{\rm End}^{0}({\Delta_{\pi}}){{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1})
, see
[
39
, Theorem 1.36]
). We are going to show that

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{f}({\rm Gal}_{L^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1}))={\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i+1}^{-1})),

then
[
39
, Remark 2.5]
) implies that
{(\Delta_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{0})}^{i+1}_{i}|_{L^{\prime}}
is always crystalline. Indeed, by
[
39
, Proposition 2.7]
), we have

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{f}({\rm Gal}_{L^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))+\dim_{E}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))/{\rm Fil}^{H}_{0}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))+\dim_{E}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L^{\prime}},{\mathbf{W}}^{0}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}^{-1})).\;

It remains to show that
(a)
and
(d)
are equivalent. Suppose there exists
i\in\Delta_{k}
such that
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}|_{L^{\prime}}
is crystalline.
N
is zero on
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}|_{L^{\prime}}
. Then we see that
N^{k-i-1}=0
on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{i+1})
,
N^{i-1}=0
on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{i-1}_{1})
and
N=0
on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i})
. We deduce from an easy dévissage argument that the monodromy operator
N^{k-1}=N^{(k-i-1)+(i-1)+1}=0
on
{\mathbf{DF}}(\pi)
. The “only if" part follows. Conversely, suppose
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}|_{L^{\prime}}
is semistable noncrystalline for all
i\in\Delta_{k}
. By definition, we pick a basis

\{v_{1,1},v_{1,2},\cdots,v_{1,r}\}\cup\cdots\cup\{v_{k,1},v_{k,2},\cdots,v_{k,r}\}\cup\{v_{k-1,1},v_{k-1,2},\cdots,v_{k-1,r}\}

of
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}})
such that the matrices of
\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}
and
N
under this basis is given by

\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}A_{1}&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&q_{L}\cdot A_{2}&\ast&\ast\\
&&\ddots&\ast\\
&&&q_{L}^{(k-1)}\cdot A_{k}\\
\end{array}\right),\;N=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&\ast&\ast&\ast\\
&0&\ast&\ast\\
&&\ddots&\ast\\
&&&0\\
\end{array}\right),

where all the
A_{i}\cong A
for some matrix
A\in{\rm GL}_{r}(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)
. We use induction on
l
to show that
N^{l-1}\neq 0
on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{l}_{1})
and
N^{l-1}v_{l,j}\neq 0
for some
1\leq j\leq r
. This is trivial for
l=2
. Suppose that
N^{l-2}\neq 0
on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{l-1}_{1})
and
N^{l-2}A_{l-1}v_{l-1,j}\neq 0
for some
j
. Then we have
\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l-1,j}-q_{L}^{l-1}A_{l-1}v_{l-1,j}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{l-2}_{1}
,
\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l,j}-q_{L}^{l}A_{l}v_{l,j}\in{\mathbf{D}}^{l-1}_{1}
and

\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}(Nv_{l,j}-v_{l-1,j})\in D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{l-2}_{1})
. We show that
N^{l-1}A_{l}v_{l,j}\neq 0
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle q_{L}^{l}N^{l-2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}NA_{l}v_{l,j}

\displaystyle=N^{l-2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}N(\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l,j}+(q_{L}^{l}A_{l}v_{l,j}-\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l,j}))

(36)

\displaystyle=N^{l-2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}N(\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l,j})=q_{L}N^{l-2}\varphi^{2f_{L}^{\prime}}Nv_{l,j}

\displaystyle=q_{L}N^{l-2}\varphi^{2f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l-1,j}=q_{L}^{2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}N^{l-2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l-1,j}

\displaystyle=q_{L}^{2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}N^{l-2}(q_{L}^{l-1}A_{l-1}v_{l-1,j}+(\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}v_{l-1,j}-q_{L}^{l-1}A_{l-1}v_{l-1,j}))

\displaystyle=q_{L}^{2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}(q_{L}^{l-1}N^{l-2}A_{l-1}v_{l-1,j})\neq 0.

Since
N^{l-2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}NA_{l}v_{l,j}=q_{L}^{l-2}\varphi^{f_{L}^{\prime}}N^{l-1}A_{l}v_{l,j}
, we get that
N^{l-1}A_{l}v_{l,j}\neq 0
and hence
N^{l-1}\neq 0
on
D_{{\rm pst}}({\mathbf{D}}^{l}_{1})
. This completes the proof.
∎

For
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, recall that
{\rm Hom}_{\tau}(L^{\times},E)
is a two-dimensional
E
-vector space and admits a basis
\psi_{\sigma,L}:=\tau\circ\log_{p}
and
\psi_{{\rm ur}}:=\mathrm{v}al_{L}
, where
\log_{p}:L^{\times}\rightarrow L
is equal to the
p
-adic logarithm when restricted to
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
and where
\log_{p}(p)
. We put
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}:=\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}\cap{\rm Hom}_{\tau}(L^{\times},E)
.

Remark 2.16
.

Assume that
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}
is potentially semistable and noncrystalline for each
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
, then the Lemma
2.15
shows that
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)\nsubseteq\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}
. Therefore, we have

\dim_{E}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}=1

for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
and
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}
. Moreover, there exists
\mathcal{L}_{i,\tau}\in E
such that
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}
is generated by
\psi_{i,\tau}:=\psi_{\tau,L}-\mathcal{L}_{i,\tau}\psi_{{\rm ur}}
.

2.5
Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula

In
[
26
, Section. 3.3]
, Ding established the Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula
(on simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants) for a rank
n
triangulable
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
. In this section, we will establish the Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula (on parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants) for a rank
n

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
, which
admits a non-critical special weakly
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
with parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
. In particular, our Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula shows that we can reinterpret the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants in terms of
1
-order paraboline deformations of type
\Omega_{[1,k]}
. The Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula also gives a way to compute the
E
-dimension of the tangent space
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
, see Proposition
2.24
.

Let
\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}
be a fixed deformation of
{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}
. We view it as a class
[\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}]
in extension group
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k-1},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})
. We assume that
\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}
admits an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{1}^{k-1}
with parameter

(({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k-1},(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i})_{1\leq i\leq k-1}),\;

i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i}\hookrightarrow{\Delta_{\pi}}\otimes_{\,\;\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i})
for all
1\leq i\leq k-1
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}={\rm gr}_{k-1}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{1}^{k-1}}\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}
be the
(k-1)
-th graded piece of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}
. By the proof of
[
12
, Proposition]
and (
29
), we see that
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{i}([\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}])\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k-1},\Delta_{\pi,k-1})
belongs to the image of
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})
via the injection

{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime}_{i}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k-1},\Delta_{\pi,k-1}).\;

Therefore, we can view
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{i}([\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}])
as an element in
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})
.

This section is devoted to proving the following theorem. The basic strategy of the proof of
[
13
, Theorem 2.7]
is also suitable for our case, but the computation is much more complicated. The proof consists of some computations of cohomology of the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. We suggest skipping the proof on the first reading.

Theorem 2.17
.

Fix the above parameters
(({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k-1},(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i})_{1\leq i\leq k-1})
. Let

\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},k}
be a deformation of
\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},k}
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}
be a deformation of
{\mathbf{D}}_{k}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}
such that

\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\hookrightarrow{\Delta_{\pi}}\otimes_{\,\;\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},k})

(so that
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\in F^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
). Then there exists a deformation
{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}
,
which admits an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
with parameter
(({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i})_{1\leq i\leq k})
and
{\rm gr}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}_{i}{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
if and only if

\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{i}([\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}])\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}({\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},k}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},k})\in\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{(k-1)r}.

Remark 2.18
.

The Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula (on parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants) shows that the parabolic simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants characterize obstructions to paraboline deformations of type
\Omega_{[1,k]}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
.

Beweis.

Replacing
{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
and
\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1}
by

respectively, we can assume that

and

). We put

By Lemma
2.7
and an easy dévissage argument, we see that
u^{\prime}_{1}
is a surjection and
u^{\prime}_{2}
is an isomorphism. Therefore, we deduce that

{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k},{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k},{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}),\;{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})

are isomorphisms by
4
-lemma. This show that
u_{2}^{\prime\prime}
is an isomorphism. We deduce from the above long exact sequence that
u_{2}
is an isomorphism. At the same time, we see that the morphism

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k},{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k},{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}),\;{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})

in
u_{1}^{\prime\prime}
are surjective by
4
-lemma. Then by an easy dévissage argument, we deduce that
u_{1}
is surjective. We conclude that
v_{1}
is a surjection by an easy diagram chasing (see (
47
)).

Note that
{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
admits a
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration with parameter
(({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i})_{1\leq i\leq k})
such that
{\rm gr}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}_{i}{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i}
if and only if
[{\mathbf{D}}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{{\mathbf{D}}}_{1}^{k-1})
lies in
\mathrm{Im}(p)
(see (
47
)), i.e.,
c([{\mathbf{D}}])=0
. The above discussion implies that
{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
admits the desired
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration if and only if
c^{\prime}([{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}])=0
, where

[{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}]=w_{1}([{\mathbf{D}}])\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})
.

Claim.
The composition
x_{3}=x_{2}\circ x_{2}^{\prime}:{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{1,2}^{\prime})
is injective.

Proof of the claim.

By the second row of (
45
) and second row of (
46
) and a diagram chasing, we get the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{(\ast\ast)}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1})\oplus{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi,E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\rm Ext}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({{\mathbf{D}}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\rm Ext}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({{\mathbf{D}}}_{k},\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1})\oplus{{\rm Ext}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})}

(52)

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{2}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{1,2}^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x_{2}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{1,2}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

(53)

The isomorphism in
(\ast\ast)
follows from Lemma
2.8
. By Lemma
2.8
, an easy dévissage argument and
4
-Lemma, we see that the morphisms

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}),\;

(54)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi,E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1})

are isomorphisms. This implies that
x_{3}
is an injection by an easy diagram chasing. This completes the proof of the claim.

Therefore, we conclude that
[{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})
lies in
\mathrm{Im}(p^{\prime})
if and only if the class
c^{\prime\prime\prime}((w_{1}^{\prime}\circ w_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}([{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}]))=0
. Recall that

\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{i}([\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}])\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k-1},\Delta_{\pi}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},k-1}))

belongs to the image of
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})
via the injection

{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime\prime}_{i}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{I}}^{\prime}_{i}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k-1},\Delta_{\pi}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},k-1})).\;

By the naturality of cup products, we deduce

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},\Delta_{\pi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{0}}
\textstyle{\times\hskip 10.0pt{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{\hskip 10.0pt{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c^{\prime\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},\Delta_{\pi})}
\textstyle{\times\hskip 10.0pt{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}

perfect
\textstyle{\hskip 10.0pt{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1}).}

This shows that the connection map
c^{\prime\prime\prime}
is given by the cup product
\langle[\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}],-\rangle
, i.e,

\langle-,-\rangle:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\times{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\Delta_{\pi}},{\Delta_{\pi}})\xrightarrow{\cup}{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{\pi},{\mathbf{D}}_{k-1})\cong E.\;

So we have
c^{\prime\prime\prime}((w_{1}^{\prime}\circ w_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}([{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}]))=0
if and only if
\langle(w_{1}^{\prime}\circ w_{1}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}([{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}]),\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{i}([\widetilde{{\mathbf{D}}}_{k-1}])\rangle=0
. Then the conclusion follows from the definition of parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants.
∎

As a corollary of Theorem
2.17
, we deduce

Corollary 2.19
.

The map
\kappa:F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\longrightarrow\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
(see (
19
)) factors through a surjective map

\kappa:F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})=\prod_{sr\in\Delta_{n}(k)}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{sr}.

We end this section with a computation of the
E
-dimension of the tangent space
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
. Unlike the generic case
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.16]
, the corollary below will show that the morphism
\Upsilon^{0}
in (
18
) is not formally smooth, so that the
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.17]
is not suitable for us. Instead, we will use Lemma
2.22
. We need some preliminaries. See (
19
) for the definition of
\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
,
\omega_{0}
and
\kappa_{L}
.

Corollary 2.20
.

The image of the map

\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}):F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

is given by
(\kappa_{L}\circ\omega^{0})^{-1}(\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}))
. In particular, we have

\dim_{E}{\rm Im}\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})=1+d_{L}(k+\frac{n(r-1)}{2})
and
\dim_{E}\mathrm{coker}(\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}))=k-1.

Beweis.

By Corollary
2.19
, we see that
{\rm Im}\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
is contained in
(\kappa_{L}\circ\omega^{0})^{-1}(\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}))
. Since
\kappa_{L}
is a surjection with kernel
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, it remains to show that

{\rm Im}\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})=(\omega^{0})^{-1}(\kappa_{L}^{-1}(\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}))).\;

For any
(\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\in\kappa_{L}^{-1}(\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}))
, and
(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\in(\omega^{0})^{-1}((\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}))\subseteq\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
, we need to show that there exists a deformation
{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}
which admits an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
with parameter
(({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\bm{\delta}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},i})_{1\leq i\leq k})
and
{\rm gr}^{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}_{i}{\mathbf{D}}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
. This can be done by using Theorem
2.17
step by step. By the proof of
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.4]
, we see that

\dim_{E}(\omega^{0})^{-1}((\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}))=k\cdot d_{L}\frac{r(r-1)}{2}=d_{L}\frac{n(r-1)}{2}.

We see that
\dim_{E}{\rm Im}\Upsilon^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})=d_{L}\frac{n(r-1)}{2}+\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)+\dim_{E}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}(k+\frac{n(r-1)}{2})
.
∎

In the sequel, we assume that
\mathcal{F}
is furthermore a non-critical special
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}
(see Definition
2.4
), i.e., we further assume that
{\mathbf{D}}^{i+1}_{i}
is non-split for all
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
. We put

{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):=\{f\in{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\;|\;f({\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\},

which is a saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
{\rm End}(D,D)
. For any
f\in{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, we see that
f({\mathbf{D}}_{i})\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
. Thus
f
produces an element
(f_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\in\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{i})
.

The following proposition computes the cohomology of
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, and gives some properties of the functor
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
.

Proposition 2.21
.

(1) We have
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))\cong E
.

(2) We have
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))=0
.

(3) The functor
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
is pro-representable and is formally smooth over
E
of dimension
1+d_{L}\frac{n(n+r)}{2}
.

Beweis.

For Part (1), it suffices to show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))\cong E
. We have an exact sequence of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
:

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow 0.

Taking cohomology, we get

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}).

Since
{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1}
is nonsplit, we deduce
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1})=0
. By Lemma
2.7
and an easy dévissage argument, we see that
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{k-1})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})
and then
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})=0
. Moreover, by Lemma
2.7
and an easy dévissage argument, we see that
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}_{k})=0
. We then see that

{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1},{\mathbf{D}})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1})).

Then Part (1) follows by induction on
k
. By
[
22
, Proposition 3.4]
, we deduce that
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
is pro-representable. For Part (2), we have a natural exact sequence of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
:

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1})\rightarrow 0,\;

where we also use
\mathcal{F}
to denote the induced
\Omega_{[1,k-1]}
-filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1}
. Applying the same strategy to
{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{s}
for
1\leq s\leq k-1
, we conclude that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is isomorphic to an extension of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}({\mathbf{D}}_{s},{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{1})
for
s=1,\cdots,k
. Therefore,
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
\frac{k(k+1)}{2}r^{2}
. By Tate duality, Lemma
2.7
and an easy dévissage argument, we have for
2\leq s\leq k
the isomorphisms:

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{s},{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{1})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{1},{\mathbf{D}}_{s}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}}))\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{s-1},{\mathbf{D}}_{s}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}}))

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{s},{\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{s-1}),

Let
M_{1}:={\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})
and let
M_{s}:=({\mathbf{D}}^{s}_{s-1})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}{\mathbf{D}}_{s}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})
for
2\leq s\leq k
, which is isomorphic to a nonsplit extension of
{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{s-1},{\mathbf{D}}_{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})
by
{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})
. We are going to show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(M_{s})=0
for
s=1,\cdots,k
. By an easy variation of the proof of Lemma
2.11
, we get
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(M_{1})=0
. For
2\leq s\leq k
, if
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(M_{s})\neq 0
, then there exists a nonzero injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules
j:\mathcal{R}_{E,L}\rightarrow M_{s}
. Since

{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})[\frac{1}{t}])={\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\Delta_{\pi}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})[\frac{1}{t}])=0\;

we see that
\mathrm{Im}(j)[\frac{1}{t}]\cap M_{s}
is a saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{s-1},{\mathbf{D}}_{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})
. By Tate duality and Lemma
2.8
, we have

{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{s-1},{\mathbf{D}}_{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}}))={\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\Delta_{\pi}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}{\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})).

Since

{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}^{0}({\Delta_{\pi}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}{\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}}))=0
, by comparing Hodge-Tate weights, we see that

\mathrm{Im}(j)[1/t]\cap M_{s}\cong\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s-1}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},s}{\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}})
. This show that

{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{s-1},{\mathbf{D}}_{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}}),M_{s})\neq 0,\;

which leads a contradiction to the fact that
M_{s}
is non-split. For Part (3), we deduce from Part (1) and
[
22
, Proposition 3.4]
that
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
is pro-representable. By Part (2) and
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.15 (2)]
, we deduce that
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}
is formally smooth of dimension
1+\frac{k(k+1)}{2}d_{L}r^{2}
. ∎

Recall that

F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}=\mathscr{F}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}\times_{\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}^{0}.

To evaluate
\dim_{E}F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
, we need a dimension formula. An easy diagram chasing shows that

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
V,V^{\prime}
and
V^{\prime\prime}
be vector spaces over
E
, and
u:V\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime}
(resp.,
u^{\prime}:V^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime}
) be
E
-linear homomorphism. Then we have

\dim_{E}V\times_{V^{\prime\prime}}V^{\prime}:=\dim_{E}V-\dim_{E}{\rm Im}u+\dim_{E}(u^{\prime})^{-1}({\rm Im}u\cap{\rm Im}u^{\prime}),

where
V\times_{V^{\prime\prime}}V^{\prime}=\{(v,v^{\prime})\in V\times V^{\prime}:u(v)=u^{\prime}(v^{\prime})\in V^{\prime\prime}\}
. Moreover,

\dim_{E}V\times_{V^{\prime\prime}}V^{\prime}=\dim_{E}V+\dim_{E}V^{\prime}-\dim_{E}V^{\prime\prime}

if and only if
\dim_{E}\mathrm{coker}(u)=\dim_{E}\mathrm{coker}(V\times_{V^{\prime\prime}}V^{\prime}\rightarrow V^{\prime})
. In particular, if one of
u,u^{\prime}
is surjective, we have
\dim_{E}V\times_{V^{\prime\prime}}V^{\prime}=\dim_{E}V+\dim_{E}V^{\prime}-\dim_{E}V^{\prime\prime}
.

Suggested by Lemma
2.22
and Corollary
2.20
, we prove (as a Corollary of Lemma
2.21
):

Corollary 2.23
.

The cokernel of the map
\Upsilon:F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
has
E
-dimensional
k-1
.

Beweis.

By induction on
k
, we get the following commutative diagram of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
:,

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}^{k-1}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Taking cohomology, we deduce from Proposition
2.21
(and the proof of Proposition
2.21
(2)) the following long exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})=0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}))\cong E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k})\cong E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{i}))\cong E^{k}}

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
