E^{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 176.56424pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 176.56424pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 279.78937pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 279.78937pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(55)

\textstyle{F_{{\mathbf{D}}^{k}_{1},\mathcal{F}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon_{k-1}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}})=0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k-1}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}_{\mathrm{Rep}_{{\mathbf{B}}_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})}({\mathbf{D}}_{k},{\mathbf{D}}_{k})=0.}

By comparing dimensions, we see that the middle three terms of
1
-th extension groups (of two rows of (
55
)) form two short exact sequences. An easy dévissage argument shows that
\Upsilon^{\prime\prime}
is injective and
\dim_{E}\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon^{\prime\prime}=1
. Then the snake lemma implies

0\rightarrow\ker\Upsilon_{k}\rightarrow\ker\Upsilon_{k-1}\rightarrow\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon_{k}\rightarrow\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon_{k-1}\rightarrow 0.

Note that
\Upsilon_{1}
is an isomorphism (so that
\ker\Upsilon_{1}=\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon_{1}=0
). By an induction on
k
, we see that
\ker\Upsilon_{k}\cong\ker\Upsilon_{k-1}\cong\cdots\cong\ker\Upsilon_{1}=0
and
\dim_{E}\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon_{k}=1+\dim_{E}\mathrm{coker}\Upsilon_{k-1}=\cdots=k-1
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 2.24
.

The functors
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
is pro-representable. Its tangent space has
E
-dimension

\dim_{E}F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})=1+d_{L}\Big(k+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\Big).

Beweis.

By
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.3]
, we get that the functor
\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\rm gr}_{i}D}^{0}
is relatively representable over
\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}
. By Proposition
2.21
(3), the functor
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
is pro-representable. By
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.4]
, we see that
\prod_{i=1}^{k}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}^{0}
is formally smooth of dimension
k\big(1+d_{L}\big(1+\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\big)\big)
. It follows from the Lemma
2.22
, Corollary
2.20
, Proposition
2.21
,
[
12
, Proposition 4.1.4]
and Corollary
2.23
that

\displaystyle\dim_{E}F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

(56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim_{E}\mathscr{F}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\dim_{E}F_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\dim_{E}F^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{i}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+d_{L}\frac{n(n+r)}{2}-k(1+d_{L}r^{2})+k\Big(1+d_{L}\Big(1+\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\Big)\Big),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+d_{L}\Big(k+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\Big).

The result follows.
∎

Remark 2.25
.

We may ask if
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
is formally smooth. The author does not know whether it is true.

3
Summary of certain locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations

Let
\underline{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
be a weight of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
. For
I\subseteq\Delta_{n}=\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
, we call that
\underline{\lambda}
is
I
-dominant with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
(resp. with respect to
\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{/E}
) if
\lambda_{i,\sigma}\geq\lambda_{i+1,\sigma}

(
resp.
\lambda_{i,\sigma}\leq\lambda_{i+1,\sigma}
)
for all
i\in I
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. We denote by
X_{I}^{+}
(resp.
X_{I}^{-}
) the set of
I
-dominant integral weights of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
with respect to
{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
(resp. with respect to
\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}_{/E}
). Note that
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
if and only if
-\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{-}
. For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
, there exists a unique irreducible algebraic representation, denoted by
L(\underline{\lambda})_{I}
, of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}
with highest weight
\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}\cap{\mathbf{B}}_{/E}
. We put
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=L(\underline{\lambda})^{\vee}_{I}
, which is an irreducible algebraic representation of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}
with highest weight
-\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{/E}\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{/E}
. Denote
\chi_{\underline{\lambda}}:=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\emptyset}
. If
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, let
L(\underline{\lambda}):=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}}
. A
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
over
E
is the induced action of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)\subset\mathbf{G}_{/E}(E)
on an algebraic representation of
\mathbf{G}_{/E}
. By abuse of notation we will use the same notations to denote
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations induced from an algebraic representation of
\mathbf{G}_{/E}
.

Let
\underline{\lambda}
be an integral weight, denote by
M(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\Sigma_{L}})}\underline{\lambda}
(resp.
\overline{M}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}(\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\Sigma_{L}})}\underline{\lambda}
), the corresponding Verma module with respect to
{\mathfrak{b}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
). Let
L(\underline{\lambda})
(resp.
\overline{L}(\underline{\lambda})
) be the unique simple quotient of
M(\underline{\lambda})
(resp. of
\overline{M}(\underline{\lambda})
). Actually, when
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
(i.e.
-\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{-}
),
L(\underline{\lambda})
is finite dimensional and isomorphic to the algebraic representation
L(\underline{\lambda})
introduced above (hence there is no conflict of notation). We have
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})\cong L(\underline{\lambda})^{\vee}
. In general, for any subset
I
of
\Delta_{n}
, and
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
, we define the generalized parabolic Verma module

\displaystyle M_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}L(\underline{\lambda})_{I},\;

(57)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}

\displaystyle\overline{M}_{I}(-\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}})\otimes_{\text{U}(\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}})}\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I})

with respect to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,\Sigma_{L}}
), see
[
35
, Chapter 9]
for more precise statements. For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}^{+}
, we put (when
I=\emptyset
, we omit the subscripts
I
in (
58
))

\displaystyle L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\;\overline{L}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda})_{I}:=\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I},\;

(58)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle M}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda}):={M}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}(\underline{\lambda}),\;\overline{M}_{I}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(-\underline{\lambda}):=\overline{{M}}_{\Delta_{n}^{k}\cup I}(-\underline{\lambda}).

3.1
General constructions

Let
\pi
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
over
E
. For any
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we put
\pi(i):=\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{i}
. Let
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)=[\pi(k-1),\cdots,\pi(1),\pi]
be a Zelevinsky-segment (see
[
46
]
). Put

\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}:=\big(\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi(k-i)\big)\otimes_{E}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{1/2}=\otimes_{i=1}^{k}\big(\pi\otimes_{E}v_{r}^{-\frac{r}{2}(k-2i+1)+k-i}\big),\;

(59)

where
\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}
is the modulus character of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)
. This is an irreducible cuspidal smooth representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
over
E
.

In the sequel, we put
G={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
for simplicity. Let
I
be a subset of
{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. By
[
46
, Proposition 2.10]
, we see that
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}(L)}\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
admits a unique irreducible subrepresentation
\pi_{I}
. Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta_{n}}^{+}
, and let
I,J\subseteq{\Delta_{n}(k)}
. We put

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathrm{Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I}\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})_{I}\Big)^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-\mathrm{an}},i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\left(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{I}\right)\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda}).

In the beginning of
[
34
, Section 4.2]
and
[
34
, Proposition 3.6]
, we show that if
J\supsetneq I
, we have an injection
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. Therefore, we put

\displaystyle v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})={\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/\sum_{J\supsetneq I}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\;

\displaystyle v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big/u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\;u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{I}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\sum_{J\supsetneq I}i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{J}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).\;

In particular, we denote by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=v^{\infty}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\emptyset}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
(resp.,
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
) the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic parabolic Steinberg representation (resp., locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic Steinberg representation) with respect to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{[k-1,0]}(\pi)
and the weight
\underline{\lambda}
. The main theorem of
[
34
]
is (see
[
34
, Theorem 5.19]
)

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
, we have an
E
-vector spaces isomorphism

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(60)

In particular, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)=d_{L}+1
.

For
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},\;E)
, the image of
\psi
via the above isomorphism (
3.1
) can be constructed explicitly as follows. Recall that we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\iota_{\upsilon}:{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)/{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}(L),E),

(61)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle[(a_{1}I_{r},\cdots,a_{k}I_{r})\mapsto\psi(a_{i}/a_{i+1})].

Therefore, for any
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},\;E)
, we choose a lift
\Psi\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)
. Then
\Psi
induces an extension
\iota(\Psi)
of
\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
by
\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}
:

\iota(\Psi)(a)=\pi^{{\langle r\rangle}}(a)\otimes_{E}\begin{pmatrix}1&\Psi\circ\det(a)\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\\
\forall\penalty\ a\in{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{J}(L).

Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction
\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\iota(\Psi)\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
, which lies in the following exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\iota(\Psi)\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\underline{\lambda})\big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\longrightarrow 0.

(62)

Pushforward (
62
) along surjection
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\twoheadrightarrow v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
and then pullback via natural injections

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

we get a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\mathscr{E}_{ir}^{\emptyset}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\Psi)^{0}
, which is an extension of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. It gives a cohomology class

[\mathscr{E}_{ir}^{\emptyset}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)^{0}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),v^{{\rm an}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)

(we can show that this cohomology class is independent on the choice of
\Psi
). By
[
34
, Theorem 5.19]
, we see that the natural map

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}))\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})),\;

induced by the surjection
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, is actually an isomorphism. Therefore, the pull back of
\mathscr{E}_{\{i\}}^{\emptyset}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\iota_{\nu}(\psi))^{0}
via the natural injection
u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\rightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{G}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is split (as an extension of
u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
). Quotient it by
u_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, we deduce
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
, which is an extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. We therefore get that
[\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
is the extension class associated with
\psi
via (
3.1
).

For
I\subset\Delta_{n}(k)
, we recall the Orlik-Strauch functor
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(-,-)
(see
[
40
, The main theorem]
), or see
[
10
, Section 2]
), which associates, to an object
M
in the Bernstein-Gelfand-Gelfand (BGG) category
\mathcal{O}_{{\rm alg}}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{{{I}}},\Sigma_{L}}
, and an finite length smooth admissible representation
\pi_{I}
, a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{{I}}}(M,\pi_{I})
.

For
ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}
,
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we put
\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma})
, and
\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma}:=(\lambda_{1,\sigma^{\prime}},\cdots,\lambda_{n,\sigma^{\prime}})_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}
. We put

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\Big(\big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}(L)}^{G}{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma}))^{\sigma-{\rm an}}\otimes_{E}L(\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma})\Big)/v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

\displaystyle C_{i,\sigma}:=\mathcal{F}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}}^{G}\big(\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda}^{\sigma})\otimes_{E}\overline{L}(-s_{ir,\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda}_{\sigma}),{\rm St}_{{\Delta}_{k,i}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{0})\big),

By
[
34
, Proposition 4.6 and Proposition 4.9]
,
C_{i,\sigma}
appears as an irreducible constituent in
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})

with multiplicity
1
. By the argument after
[
34
, (5.48)]
,
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is a subrepresentation of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
, and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\sigma}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
admits a subrepresentation
\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
which is an extension of
C_{i,\sigma}
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. We put

\displaystyle\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\bigoplus^{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}\Sigma_{ir,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})=\bigoplus^{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),

(63)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\bigoplus^{ir\in{\Delta_{n}(k)}}_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\bigoplus^{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})}\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\subseteq{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}).

By the definition,
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
is an extension of
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}C_{i,\sigma}
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
. Moreover, we have a natural isomorphism (see
[
34
, Proposition 5.34]
)

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big).

(64)

For any
\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, let
[\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{{ir}}}^{\infty}(\pi,\underline{\lambda}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\big)
be the
image of
\psi
via the isomorphism (
64
). Moreover, we can see that
[\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
actually comes from
[\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)]
by pushing-forward
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\hookrightarrow{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
.

Let
\alpha\in E^{\times}
, we denote
\ast(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda}):=\ast(\pi,\underline{\lambda})\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\alpha)\circ\det
for any representation
\ast(\pi,\underline{\lambda})
of
G
as above (for example,
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda})
,
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda})
,
\widetilde{{\Sigma}}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi)
, etc.).

For
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
(so for
1\leq i\leq k-1
), let
V_{i}
be an
E
-vector subspace of
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
of dimension
d_{L}
. Let
\{\psi_{i,1},\cdots,\psi_{i,d_{L}}\}
be a basis of
V_{i}
. We put

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\underline{\lambda},V_{i})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda})}^{j=1,\cdots,d_{L}}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi_{i,j}),

\displaystyle\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V_{i})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda})}^{j=1,\cdots,d_{L}}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},\psi_{i,j}).

Thus
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V_{i})
is a subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V_{i})
, both of the representations are independent of the choice of the basis of
V_{i}
and determine
V_{i}
. Put
V:=\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}V_{i}
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda})}^{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V_{i}),

\displaystyle\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda})}^{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V_{i}).

It is clear that
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V)
is a subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,\underline{\lambda},V)
.

3.2
A subcandidate in the locally analytic
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-adic local Langlands program

The main result of this section is given as follows. Let
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
be a potentially semistable representation, and let
{\mathbf{D}}=D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
be the associated

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
n
.

Let
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be the Hodge-Tate weights of
\rho_{L}
(or
{\mathbf{D}}
). We put
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
. We put
{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}
, which is a dominant weight of
(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\rm GL}_{n})\times_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
respect to
(\mathrm{Res}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}E
.

Suppose that
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a non-critical special
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration (see Definition
2.4
) with
parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},{\bm{\delta}_{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
(or
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
).

Many information on
\rho_{L}
is lost when passing from
\rho_{L}
to its associated Weil-Deligne representation
\mathrm{WD}(\rho_{L})
. We have defined the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})
of
\rho_{L}
. Then we will see that the locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha_{\pi},\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha_{\pi},\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))
of
G
carry the exact information on the Weil-Deligne representation
{\textbf{WD}}(\rho_{L})\cong{\textbf{WD}}({\mathbf{D}})
associated with
\rho_{L}
, the Hodge-Tate weights
\mathrm{HT}(\rho_{L})
of
\rho_{L}
, and the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})
of
\rho_{L}
. Both of the representations determine exactly the data
\{{\textbf{WD}}(\rho_{L}),\mathrm{HT}(\rho_{L}),\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})\}
, and vice versa.

Proposition 3.2
.

We have:

(1)

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))
is isomorphic to an extension of
\bigoplus\limits_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{d_{L}}
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
.

(2)

199.50528pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-199.50528pt\ignorespaces\kern 199.9053pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-199.9053pt\ignorespaces\kern 200.30533pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-200.30533pt\ignorespaces\kern 200.70535pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-200.70535pt\ignorespaces\kern 201.10538pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-201.10538pt\ignorespaces\kern 201.5054pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-201.5054pt\ignorespaces\kern 201.90543pt\raise-0.30556pt\hbox{\fiverm.}\kern-201.90543pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\kern 171.91049pt\raise 3.00018pt\hbox{}\kern-171.91049pt\ignorespaces
}\kern 274.13808pt},

(65)

(3)

{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir})\cong{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
.

(4)

The locally algebraic subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))
(resp. of
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir})
for
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
) is isomorphic to
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
.

Beweis.

Part (1) is clear.  For (2), note that the Remark
2.16
asserts that
\dim_{E}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}=1
for all
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, and
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}
. Thus, there exists a
\mathcal{L}_{i,\tau}\in E
such that
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir,\tau}
is generated by
\psi_{i,\tau}:=\psi_{\tau,L}-\mathcal{L}_{i,\tau}\psi_{{\rm ur}}
. Then
\{\psi_{i,\tau}\}_{i\in\Delta_{n}(k),\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
form a basis of
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}})_{ir}
. By the same argument as in the the discussion after
[
26
, Lem. 3.3]
, we can obtain the decomposition

{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi_{i,\tau})={\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{i,\tau}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi_{i,\tau})\oplus_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})}\left(\bigoplus_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})}^{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L},\sigma\neq\tau}\Sigma_{i,\sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\right).

This deduces (2). Part (3) is a direct consequence of
[
34
, Proposition 5.28]
. By
[
34
, Remark 5.21]
, we see that the locally algebraic subrepresentation of
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir})
is strictly bigger than

{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
if and only if
{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir}
. Then
(4)
follows from Lemma
2.15
and Remark
2.16
.
∎

4
Local-global compatibility

In this Chapter, we prove some new (
p
-adic) local-global compatibility results for potentially semistable non-crystalline
p
-adic Galois representation.

Since we want to explore the non-trianguline Galois representations, our local-global compatibility results are realized in the framework of patched Bernstein eigenvariety (roughly speaking, since we can only see finite slope
p
-adic automorphism forms or trianguline representation in the classical eigenvarieties), which is constructed by Christophe Breuil and Yiwen Ding (see
[
12
]
).

We briefly describe the contents of each section. In Section
4.1
, we first review the global patching construction (hence we also assume the so-called Talyor-Wiles hypothesis as in
[
21
]
). Then we construct the patched Bernstein eigenvariety (by an easy variation of
[
12
, Section 3.3]
) and the (purely local) Bernstein paraboline varieties (see
[
12
, Section 4.2]
).

To state our local-global compatibility results, let
x
be a point in the patched Bernstein eigenvariety such that the associated
p
-adic Galois representation
\rho_{x}
admits a non-critical special
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration.

In Section
4.4
, we show that
x
is a smooth point of the patched Bernstein eigenvariety. By Section
2.4
, we can attach to
x
(resp.,
\rho_{L}
) the parabolic Fontaine-Mazur simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})
. We further show that the tangent map of the “weight" map at point
x
recovers the information of
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})
(see Proposition
4.11
). Via a study of parabolic Breuil’s simple
\mathcal{L}
-invariants (Sections
3
), we attach to
\rho_{L}
a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))
. The second main theorem (see Section
4.5
, our
p
-adic local-global compatibility results) of this paper asserts that
\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\pi,\underline{\lambda},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L}))
is a subrepresentation of the associated Hecke-isotypic subspaces of the Banach spaces of
p
-adic automotphic form on certain (definite) unitary group (see Theorem
4.18
).

4.1
Patched Bernstein eigenvarieties and Bernstein parabolic varieties

Our Local-global compatibility results are realized in the space of the patched
p
-adic automorphic forms. More precisely, it is realized in the setting of
[
26
, Section 4.1.1]
. In this section, we recall briefly the patched Bernstein eigenvariety and Bernstein paraboline variety of Breuil-Ding (see
[
12
, Section 3.3, Section 4.2]
). Indeed, the patched arguments we need are slightly different from that in

[
12
, Section 3.3]
). We instead only vary all weights and levels at only one
p
-adic place. But the arguments in
[
12
, Section 3.3, Section 4.2]
can easily be adapted to our case.

We follow the notation of
[
26
, Section 4.1.1]
and
[
21
, Section 2]
. Suppose that
p\nmid 2n
, and let
\overline{r}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
be a continuous representation such that
\overline{r}
admits a potentially crystalline lift
r_{\mathrm{pot.diag}}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
of regular weight
\xi
which is potentially diagonalisable. We can find a triple
(F,F^{+},\overline{\rho})
, where
F
is an imaginary CM field with maximal totally real subfield
F^{+}
, and
\overline{\rho}:{\rm Gal}_{F^{+}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{n}(k_{E})
is a
suitable globalisation
(cf.
[
21
, Section 2.1]
) of
\overline{r}
. In particular, for any place
v|p
of
F^{+}
,
v
splits in
F
, and has
F^{+}_{v}\cong L
. There is a place
\widetilde{v}
of
F
lying over
v
with
\overline{\rho}|_{{\rm Gal}_{F_{\widetilde{v}}}}\cong\overline{r}
.

Let
S_{p}
be the set of places of
F^{+}
above
p
. By
[
21
, Section 2.3]
, we can find the following objects

\{\widetilde{G},v_{1},{\mathfrak{p}}\in S_{p},\{U_{m}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\},

where
\widetilde{G}
is a certain definite unitary group over
F^{+}
,
v_{1}
is a certain finite place of
F^{+}
prime to
p
, and
\{U_{m}=\prod_{v}U_{m,v}\}_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}
is a tower of certain compact open subgroups of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}}^{\infty})
(see
[
26
, Section 4.1.1, Page 8028]
or
[
21
, Section 2, Page 214]
for a precise description). We mention that
U_{m}
has full level
{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}
(resp., level
\widetilde{G}(\mathcal{O}_{F^{+}_{v}})
) at
{\mathfrak{p}}
(resp.,
v\in S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
).

Let
\tau
be the inertial type of
r_{\mathrm{pot.diag}}
. As in
[
26
, Section 4.1.1, Page 8029]
or
[
21
, Section 2, Page 215]
, we consider the space of
p
-adic automorphic forms
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})
and
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)
(roughly speaking, the space of
p
-adic algebraic automorphic forms of fixed type
\sigma(\tau)
(see
[
21
, Theorem 3.7]
, the “inertial local Langlands correspondence") at the place
S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, full level at
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and whose weight is
0
at places above
{\mathfrak{p}}
, and given by the regular weight
\xi
at each of the places in
S_{p}\setminus\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
). Note that
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)
is a Banach space for the supermum norm and is equipped with a continuous (unitary) action of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
(by right translation on functions). The space
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)
is also equipped with a faithful action of a certain commutative global Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
which is generated by some prime-to-
p
Hecke operators (see
[
26
, Section 4.1.1, Page 8029]
). We can associate to
\overline{\rho}
a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}
of
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
. Let
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\ast)_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
be the localization of
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\ast)
at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}
for
\ast\in\{\mathcal{O}_{E},E\}
.
Then the action of
{\mathbf{T}}^{S_{p},{\rm univ}}
on the localization
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
factors through certain Hecke algebra

{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}^{S_{p}}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
(see
[
26
, Page 8029]
). We also see that
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{*}
with
*\in\{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}},\emptyset\}
are admissible unitary Banach representation of with invariant lattice
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{*}
.

We denote by
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}
the maximal reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of the universal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lifting ring of
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{v}}:=\overline{\rho}|_{{\rm Gal}_{F_{\widetilde{v}}}}
(
\cong\overline{r}
, and therefore
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}\cong{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
). For
v\in S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}
, we denote by
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}
for the reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}
corresponding to potentially crystalline lifts of weight
\xi
and inertial type
\tau
. Consider the following global deformation problem (in the terminology of
[
23
]
, see also
[
21
, Section 2.4]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{{F}/{F}^{+},T^{+},T,\mathcal{O}_{E},\overline{\rho},\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{1-n}\delta_{{F}/{F}^{+}}^{n},\{R_{\widetilde{v}_{1}}^{\square}\}\cup\{R_{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\square}\}\cup\{R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}\}_{v\in S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}}\bigg\}

Then by
[
23
, Proposition 2.2.9]
(see also
[
21
, Section 2.4]
), this deformation problem is represented by a universal deformation ring
R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}
. Note that we have a natural morphism
R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}\rightarrow{\mathbf{T}}_{\xi,\tau}^{S_{p}}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{E})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
.

Following
[
26
, Section 4.1.1]
(or
[
21
, Section 2.8]
) we put

\displaystyle R^{{\rm loc}}:=R_{\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\square}\widehat{\otimes}\Big(\widehat{\otimes}_{S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}}R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}\Big)\widehat{\otimes}R_{\widetilde{v_{1}}}^{\square},

where all completed tensor products are taken over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. We put
g:=q-[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
, where
q\geq[F^{+}:{\mathbb{Q}}]\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
is a certain integer as in
[
21
, Section 2.6, Page 217]
). We now put

\displaystyle R_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle R^{{\rm loc}}\llbracket x_{1},\cdots,x_{g}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle S_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{E}\llbracket z_{1},\cdots,z_{n^{2}(|S_{p}|+1)},y_{1},\cdots,y_{q}\rrbracket,

where
x_{i}
,
y_{i}
,
z_{i}
are formal variables. By the end of
[
26
, Section 4.1.1]
(or
[
21
, Section 2.8]
), we get the following objects:

1.

a continuous
R_{\infty}
-admissible unitary representation
\Pi_{\infty}
of
G={\rm GL}_{n}(L)
over
E
together with a
G
-stable and
R_{\infty}
-stable unit ball
\Pi_{\infty}^{o}\subset\Pi_{\infty}
;

2.

a morphism of local
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-algebras
S_{\infty}{\longrightarrow}R_{\infty}
such that
M_{\infty}:={\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{o},\mathcal{O}_{E})
is finite projective as
S_{\infty}\llbracket{\rm GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\rrbracket
-module;

3.

a closed ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
R_{\infty}
, a surjection
R_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{a}}R_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}
and a
G\times R_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{a}}R_{\infty}
-invariant isomorphism
\Pi_{\infty}[{\mathfrak{a}}]\cong\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
, where
R_{\infty}
acts on
\widehat{S}_{\xi,\tau}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},E)_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\rho}}}
via
R_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{a}}R_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow R_{\mathcal{S}}^{{\rm univ}}
.

Let
R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}=\Big(\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S_{p}\backslash\{{\mathfrak{p}}\}}R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square,\xi,\tau}\Big)\widehat{\otimes}R_{\widetilde{v_{1}}}^{\square}
and
R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}:=R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\llbracket x_{1},\cdots,x_{g}\rrbracket
. Then we have
R^{{\rm loc}}=R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
(recall that
R_{\widetilde{v}}^{\square}\cong{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
by definition) and
R_{\infty}=R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
. Let
{\mathbb{U}}
be the open unit ball in
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. We put
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}:=({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R^{{\mathfrak{p}}})^{{\rm rig}}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}=({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box})^{{\rm rig}}
. Then we have
({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}
. We have thus

{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}:=({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\cong({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\cong{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}
.

Let
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be a strictly
\Delta_{n}^{k}
-dominant weight. We put
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
and put
{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}
. We denote by
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}
the subrepresentation of
G
of locally
R_{\infty}
-analytic vectors of
\Pi_{\infty}
(see
[
16
, Section 3.1]
). Recall that
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\cong Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}\times{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
, where

Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}
is the image of
\oplus_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\;(m_{i})\mapsto(\varpi_{L}^{m_{i}})
.

We recall the construction of certain
R_{\infty}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\times Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}\times{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
-module
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
in
[
12
, Sections 3.1.1, 3.1.2, 3.3]
. Using Bushnell-Kutzko’s theory of type, we can construct an absolutely irreducible smooth representation
\sigma
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
over
E
from a maximal simple type of
\Omega_{[1,k]}
(see the argument in
[
12
, Sections 3.1.1]
). We have

{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\cong{\rm End}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\mathrm{c-ind}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\sigma\big),\;

where “
\mathrm{c-ind}
” denotes the compact induction. We put

\displaystyle B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}):=

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}\big(\sigma,J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)\big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(c-\mathrm{ind}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\sigma,J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)\big),\;

where
J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}:={\rm Hom}_{{\mathfrak{d}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}(L^{{\langle r\rangle}}({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}))
(recall that
{\mathfrak{d}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
is the Lie algebra of the derived subgroup
{\mathbf{D}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
, and
J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}
is the Emerton-Jacquet functor
[
27
]
).

As in
[
12
, Sections 3.1.2]
, we recall various group actions on
B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
. We write

\displaystyle\iota_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))\cong{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\;\big(\text{i.e., the notation $\mathcal{Z}_{1}$ in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Ding2021}{}{}, (3.4)]}})

(66)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}

\displaystyle\iota_{0}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))\cong{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),\;\big(\text{i.e., the notation $\mathcal{Z}_{0}$ in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Ding2021}{}{}, (3.4)]}}\big)

for the action of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
on
B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
induced by the action
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
(resp.,
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
) on

J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}
( resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)
).

Next, the Bernstein centre
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
acts on the module
B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
via the factor
c-\mathrm{ind}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\sigma
, which commutes with the action of
\iota_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))\times\iota_{0}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
. Write
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
(resp.,
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
) for the action
\iota_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))
(resp.,
\iota_{0}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
).

We write
\Delta_{0}
for the action of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
on
B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
induced by the diagonal action of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
on
J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)
(see the argument before
[
12
, (3.4)]
). The argument before
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.2]
shows that the action of
\Delta_{0}
is determined by
\Delta_{0}|_{Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}}
(and
\Delta_{0}|_{{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}
acts via the central character of
\sigma
).

The
R_{\infty}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\times Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}\times{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
-module structure of
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}
is given by the
R_{\infty}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\times\iota_{1}(Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}})\times\iota_{0}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
-action, where
R_{\infty}
acts on
B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
via the factor
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}
. By
[
12
, Lemma 3.17]
, the
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\times Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}\times{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
-module
B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
does not depend on the choice
\sigma
. We denote hence
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}):=B_{\sigma,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
.

We also recall how to recover the action
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
from
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
-action and
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
-action. Denoting by
\psi_{\sigma}
the central character of
\sigma
(a character of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
). By
[
12
, (3.5)]
, we see that

\mathcal{Y}_{1}(z_{0})=\psi_{\sigma}(z_{0})\mathcal{Y}_{0}(\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(z_{0})).\;

for any
z_{0}\in{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
. On the other hand, we see that
\mathcal{Y}_{1}|_{Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}}=\Delta_{0}|_{Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}}
. Moreover, the
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
-action on
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\widehat{S}(U^{{\mathfrak{p}}},W^{{\mathfrak{p}}})_{\overline{\rho}}^{{\rm an}})
induced by the map

{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\rightarrow{\rm End}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\mathrm{c-ind}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}^{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\sigma\big)\cong{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}

coincides with the
\Delta_{0}
-action. Then the action
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
can be recovered from the
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
-action.

By an easy variation of the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.3]
and the argument before
[
12
, (3.28)]
, we see that
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}
is a coadmissible module over
\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\widehat{Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}})
, which corresponds to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty,0}
over
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\widehat{Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
. Since the action of
Z^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\varpi_{L}}
factors through
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}
, we see that
\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty,0}
gives rise to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}
over
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
such that

\Gamma\Big({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}\Big)\cong B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
be the Zariski-closed support of
\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}
. We call
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
the patched Bernstein eigenvariety.

By an easy variation of the proof of Proposition 3.3.2, Corollary  3.3.3, Proposition 3.3.4, Theorem 3.3.5 and Proposition 3.3.6 in
[

]
, we have

Proposition 4.1
.

(1)

For
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\pi_{x},\chi_{x})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
,
x\in\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
if and only if

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(\pi_{x}\otimes_{E}((\chi_{x})_{\varpi_{L}}\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)})\otimes_{E}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}])\Big)\neq 0

(2)

The rigid space

is reduced and equidimensional of dimension

.

(4)

The set of very classical non-critical generic points (see

and is an accumulation set. The set of very classical non-critical generic points accumulates at point
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\pi_{x},\chi_{x})
with
\chi_{x}
locally algebraic.

We now recall the definition of Bernstein paraboline varieties

, Section 4.2]

(1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}}\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}((\delta_{i}^{0})^{-1}_{\varpi_{L}})\hookrightarrow\Delta_{x_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}}\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{ir}})

(67)

Remark 4.3
.

In general,

The following definition is a generalization of the accumulation property in
[
15
, Definition 2.11]
.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
X
be a union of irreducible components of an open subset of
{X_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
. We say that
X
satisfies the accumulation property at
x
, if for any positive real number
C>0
, the set of potentially crystalline strictly points
x^{\prime}=(\rho^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime},\delta^{0}_{x^{\prime}})\in{X_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
such that:

•

x^{\prime}
is generic;

•

x^{\prime}
is non-critical;

•

{\rm wt}(\delta_{x,i}^{0})-{\rm wt}(\delta_{x,i+1}^{0})>C
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
and
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
;

accumulate at
x
.

The (purely local) Bernstein paraboline variety is closely related to the (global) patched Bernstein eigenvariety. Consider the composition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}

\displaystyle\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}

(69)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}}{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}.

where
\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}:{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}
is the isomorphism such that

\pi_{\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)_{i}}=\pi_{x_{i}}\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(q_{L}^{(i-1)r+\frac{r-1}{2}})\circ\det

for
x=(x_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}

, Theorem 3.3.9]

Proposition 4.5
.

) factors through

4.2
Non-critical special point

Let
\rho_{L}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
be a potentially semistable non-crystalline representation. Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be the associated

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank

.

Let

(or

). We put
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}

Suppose that

admits a non-critical special
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
with parameter

(or

2.2

Recall that
\pi\cong\pi_{0}\otimes{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi})
for some
\alpha_{\pi}\in E^{\times}
. We put

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle(\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi},\underline{\mathbf{1}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},

(71)

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi}=(\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},\;\text{and\;}\pi_{\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi,i}}\cong\pi_{0}{\otimes_{E}}{\rm unr}\big(\alpha_{\pi}q_{L}^{i-k+\frac{1-r}{2}-r(i-1)}\big)\circ\det\text{\;for\;}1\leq i\leq k;\hskip 40.0pt

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle(\widehat{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi},\underline{\mathbf{1}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi}=({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},\text{and\;}\pi_{\widehat{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,i}}\cong\pi_{0}{\otimes_{E}}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{\pi}q_{L}^{i-k})\circ\det\text{\;for\;}1\leq i\leq k.\;

Suppose that there exists
x^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
such that

x=(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\rho_{L},\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi},\underline{\mathbf{1}})\in{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\;

By definition, we see that

\Lambda(x)=(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}(x_{L}))=(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}(\rho_{L},\widehat{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi},\mathbf{\underline{1}}))\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}({X_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})})

via the injection (

). In this case, we call

4.3
Accumulation property at non-critical special point

Let
\mathcal{E}(x)
be the union of irreducible components of
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
containing
x
, which is thus equidimensional of dimension

Since
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}

(by Proposition

(2)), one has

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})},x}\cong\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{E}(x),x}.\;

(72)

By Theorem
4.5
,
\mathcal{E}(x)
has the form
\cup_{i,j}\big(X_{i}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}(X_{j,{\mathfrak{p}}})\big)

is smooth at

, and hence

is a singleton

. Therefore, we have

\mathcal{E}(x)=X^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}(X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x))\hookrightarrow X^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}({X_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})})

(73)

with
X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)=\cup_{j}X_{j,{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)
satisfies the accumulation property at
x_{L}
.

Beweis.

We follow the route of
[
15
, Proposition 3.10]
, the proof of
[
16
, Theorem 3.9]
and the statement in
[
12
, Theorem 3.3.5]
. Recall that

and accumulate at any point
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\pi_{x},\chi_{x})\in{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
with
\chi_{x}
locally algebraic. We adapt the proof of this statement to our setting. More precisely, for the non-critical point
x=(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\rho_{L},\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi},\underline{\mathbf{1}})\in{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}

and an irreducible component
X
of
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
containing
x
,
one can choose an affinoid and connected neighborhood of
x
such that
U\rightarrow\omega_{X}(U)
is a finite surjective morphism, and
\omega_{X}(U)
is an affinoid open subset, by
[
12
, Proposition 3.3.2 (2)]
. For any sufficiently large

, we define a part

of

{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
consists of dominant algebraic character
\delta^{0}\in{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
such that
{\rm wt}(\delta_{\tau,i}^{0})-{\rm wt}(\delta_{\tau,i+1}^{0})>C
for
1\leq i\leq k-1
and
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
. Since
U
is affinoid, by
[
12
, Proposition 3.2.14]
and
[
12
, Proposition 3.2.9 and (3.17)]
, for any sufficiently large

, if a point

is a very classical non-critical generic point. Then by a similar argument of the second paragraph in the proof of

, Theorem 3.9]
, we see that there exists an affinoid neighbourhood

of

in

and a part

consists of very classical non-critical generic points, where

which is defined in the
[

, discussion before Proposition 3.3.2]
. Using the isomorphism

\mathcal{E}(x)=X^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}(X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)),\;

this lemma is contained in the above discussion.
∎

Let
r^{{\rm univ}}
be the universal framed Galois deformation of
\overline{r}
over

,

be the universal character of

-adic differential equation over

, Section 2.2]
). Let
