be a subspace, and let
r^{{\rm univ}}_{X}
,
\delta_{X,i}^{0,{\rm univ}}
and
\Delta^{{\rm univ}}_{\Omega_{r},X}
be the pull-back of
r^{{\rm univ}}
,
\delta_{i}^{0,{\rm univ}}
and
\Delta^{{\rm univ}}_{\pi}
over
X
. Let
D_{{\rm rig}}\big(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X}\big)
be the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{X,L}
associated to
r^{{\rm univ}}_{X}
.

For
1\leq i\leq k
, we set

\Delta_{X,i}=\Delta^{{\rm univ}}_{\Omega_{r},X}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{X,L}}\mathcal{R}_{X,L}\Big(\big(\delta_{X,i}^{0,{\rm univ}}\big)_{\varpi_{L}}z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{(i-1)r+1}}\Big).

By the argument after (
14
), we have natural injection
\Delta_{X,x_{L},i}\hookrightarrow{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\mathbf{D}}\cong{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})
for
1\leq i\leq k
, where
\Delta_{X,x_{L},i}
is the specialization of
\Delta_{X,i}
at
x_{L}
. We show that the
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration on
x_{L}
can extend to some open affinoid neighborhood around
x_{L}
.

Theorem 4.7
.

There exists an open affinoid neighborhood
X\subset X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)
of non-critical special point
x_{L}
such that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X})[\frac{1}{t}]
over
\mathcal{R}_{X,L}
admits a filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
such that
{\rm gr}^{i}\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}\cong\Delta_{X,i}[\frac{1}{t}]
. In particular, the specialization of the filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
on non-critical special point
x_{L}
gives an
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration of
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})[\frac{1}{t}]
with parameter
({{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})
.

Beweis.

By the accumulation property at
x_{L}
, there exists a Zariski dense set
S
of potentially crystalline and non-critical generic points such that for
z\in S
, we have (see the proof of
[
12
, Proposition 4.2.7]
)

\dim_{k(z)}{\rm Ext}^{i}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,z,1},D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{$i=0$;}\\
1+nr\cdot d_{L},&\hbox{$i=1$;}\\
0,&\hbox{$i=2$.}\end{array}\right.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.8
(recall that
{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/\Delta_{X,x_{L},1}
is a torsion
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module), we can deduce that for
s>1
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,x_{L},1},{\rm gr}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))=0
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,x_{L},1},{\rm gr}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))=0
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,x_{L},1},{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/\Delta_{X,x_{L},1})=0
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,x_{L},1},{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/\Delta_{X,x_{L},1})=0
.

An easy computation and dévissage argument imply

\dim_{k(x_{L})}{\rm Ext}^{i}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,x_{L},1},D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{$i=0$;}\\
1+nr\cdot d_{L},&\hbox{$i=1$;}\\
0,&\hbox{$i=2$.}\end{array}\right.

By
[
2
, Corollary 4.7]
,
{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,1},D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X}))
is locally free of rank one at
x_{L}
. Shrinking
X
, we can assume that
{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\Delta_{X,1},D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X}))
is free of rank one at
x_{L}
. A choice of a generator allows a map

\iota_{1}:\Delta_{X,1}\longrightarrow D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X}).

By
[
2
, Lemma 5.4 (a)]
,
\iota_{1}
is injective.

Now consider
Q_{X,1}:=\rm coker\iota_{1}
, which is a generalized
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{X,L}
. By
[
2
, Lemma 5.4 (b)]
, we can assume
Q_{X,1}
is nearly flat (in the sense of the
[
2
, Definition 4.1]
) by shrinking
X
. A difference with trianguline case is that the map
\iota_{1}
is never saturated, and so
Q_{X,1}
has
t
-torsions at all points. For any
z\in S
(resp.,
x_{L}
), by definition
Q_{X,z}
(resp.,
Q_{X,x_{L},1}
) is an extension of

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z}):=D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})/{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})\;

(74)

\displaystyle(\text{resp.},

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}):=D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))

by torsion
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})/\Delta_{X,z,1}
(resp.,
{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/\Delta_{X,x_{L},1}
). Moreover, as
z\in S
is generic, we have

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z}),{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})/\Delta_{X,z,1})=0

and hence
Q_{X,z,1}\cong{\rm Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})\oplus{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})/\Delta_{X,z,1}
.

For the point
x_{L}
, since
Q_{X,x_{L}}[\frac{1}{t}]={\rm Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})[\frac{1}{t}]
, then there exists a sufficiently large integer
N
, such that (using
E
-
B
-pairs and the same strategy as in the proof of Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.8
)

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},Q_{X,x_{L},1})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},{\rm Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})).

In this case, note that for
s>2
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},{\rm gr}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))=0
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},{\rm gr}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))=0
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},{\rm gr}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/\Delta_{X,x_{L},2})=0
,

-

{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},{\rm gr}_{2}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}})/\Delta_{X,x_{L},2})=0
.

We can obtain

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,x_{L},2},{\rm Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,x_{L}}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{$i=0$;}\\
1+(k-1)r^{2}\cdot d_{L},&\hbox{$i=1$;}\\
0,&\hbox{$i=2$.}\end{array}\right.

(75)

Fix this integer
N
, for any
z\in S
, we claim that if
{\rm wt}(\delta_{1,z}^{0})-{\rm wt}(\delta_{2,z}^{0})>N
, then we have

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,z,2},{\rm gr}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z})/\Delta_{X,z,1})=0.

Therefore, we can obtain

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{i}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,z,2},Q_{X,z,1})={\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,z,2},{\rm Fil}_{2}^{k}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X,z}))

(76)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{$i=0$;}\\
1+(k-1)r^{2}\cdot d_{L},&\hbox{$i=1$;}\\
0,&\hbox{$i=2$.}\end{array}\right.

By the accumulation property at
x_{L}
, the potentially crystalline and non-critical generic points
z\in S
such that
{\rm wt}(\delta_{1,z}^{0})-{\rm wt}(\delta_{2,z}^{0})>N
is Zariski dense in
X
. By
[
2
, Corollary 5.4(b)]
, we see that

{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,2},Q_{X,1})
is locally free of rank one at
x_{L}
. By shrinking
X
, we can further assume that
{\rm Ext}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(t^{N}\Delta_{X,2},Q_{X,1})
is free of rank one at
x_{L}
. A choice of a generator allows a map

\iota_{2}:t^{N}\Delta_{X,2}\longrightarrow Q_{X,1}.

By
[
2
, Corollary 5.4(a)]
,
\iota_{2}
is injective. Now consider
Q_{X,2}:=\rm coker\iota_{2}
. Proceeding as we did for
Q_{X,1}:=\rm coker\iota_{1}
, we complete the proof step by step. In conclusion, the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X})[\frac{1}{t}]
over
\mathcal{R}_{X,L}
admits a filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
such that
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}D_{{\rm rig}}(r^{{\rm univ}}_{X})[\frac{1}{t}]\cong\Delta_{X,i}[\frac{1}{t}]
. The specialization of the filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
at non-critical special point
x_{L}
gives a
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration of
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})[\frac{1}{t}]
such that

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho)[\frac{1}{t}]\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta_{\widehat{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,i}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{(i-1)r+1}})[\frac{1}{t}]\cong\Delta_{\widehat{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,i}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}})[\frac{1}{t}].

(77)

This proves the last assertion.
∎

4.4
Smoothness of non-critical special point

Recall that the deformation theory of Galois representations is usually studied by considering a functor whose values are the set of isomorphism classes of liftings. In some cases, it is better to consider a functor with values in groupoids, i.e., to consider the category of liftings and the isomorphisms between them. If
X
is a groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
, we denote by
|X|
the functor on
{\rm Art}_{E}
such that
|X|(A)
is the set of isomorphim classes of the category
X(A)
. In this section, we study certain groupoids or functors whose values are deformations of type
\Omega_{[1,k]}
for
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
(and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{D}}[\frac{1}{t}]
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[\frac{1}{t}]
).

We also fix the
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
with non-critical special parameter

({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}.\;

By inverting
t
, this filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
induces an increasing filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}=\{{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}[\frac{1}{t}]\}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
by
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[\frac{1}{t}]
. Under the terminology in
[
12
, Section 6.2]
, a parameter of
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
is
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
. By
[
12
, Lemmma 6.2.1]
, we see that all parameters of
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
are of the form
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi}^{\prime},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime})
such that, for
i=1,\cdots,k
,
\Delta_{{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,i}^{\prime}}=\Delta_{{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\psi_{i})
and
\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}^{\prime}=\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}\psi_{i}^{-1}\eta_{i}z^{\mathbf{k}}
for some unramified character
\psi_{i}
of
L^{\times}
,
\eta_{i}\in\mu_{\Omega_{\pi}}
, and
{\mathbf{k}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{|\Sigma_{L}|}
.

Let
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformation of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
, and
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of
\Omega_{[1,k]}
- deformation of
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet})
(see
[
12
, Section 6.2]
). There is a natural morphism (by forgetting the filtration)
X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}
. Let
X_{{\mathbf{D}}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
of deformation of
{\mathbf{D}}
. Recall that we have natural morphism
X_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}

by inverting
t
. More precisely, note that
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
is the deformation functor

|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|:{\rm Art}_{E}:=\{\text{Artinian local $E$-algebra with residue field $E$}\}\longrightarrow\{\text{sets}\}

sends
A
to the set of isomorphism classes
\{(\mathcal{M}_{A},j_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet})\}/\sim
, where

(1)

\mathcal{M}_{A}
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
n
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
with an isomorphism
j_{A}:\mathcal{M}_{A}\otimes_{A}E\cong\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
,

(2)

\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
is an increasing
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[\frac{1}{t}]
on
\mathcal{M}_{A}
,

(3)

j_{A}
induces isomorphisms
j_{A}:\mathcal{M}_{A,i}\otimes_{A}E\cong\mathcal{M}_{i}
.

By
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.2]
, there exists a unique characters
\delta_{A}=\otimes_{i=1}^{k}{\delta}_{A,i}
such that
{\delta}_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}(\mod\mathfrak{m}_{A})
and

({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi}:=({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi,i})_{1\leq i\leq k},\delta_{A})
is a parameter of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
, i.e., there exists an isomorphism of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}[\frac{1}{t}]
:
{\rm gr}_{i}\mathcal{M}_{A}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\Delta_{\pi}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A,i})[\frac{1}{t}]
. We put
X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}:=X_{{\mathbf{D}}}\times_{X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}}X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
.

We view the character
\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
as a point of
\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}
. Observe that the functor

A\in{\rm Art}_{E}\mapsto\{\delta_{A}:{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\rightarrow A^{\times},\;{\delta}_{A,i}\equiv\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}},i}(\mathrm{mod}\;\mathfrak{m}_{A})\}

is pro-representable by
\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
, where
\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
is the completion of
\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}
at the point
\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}
. Then we have a morphism of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}
:

\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}:X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}},\;\;(A,\mathcal{M}_{A},j_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet})\mapsto\delta_{A},

(78)

by
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.2]
.

The
E
-linear map
\delta_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}\mapsto(\delta_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}^{-1}-1)/\epsilon
induces an isomorphism

\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)=\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E).

Therefore, we
consider the following composition

\omega^{\kappa}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}:X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{L}}\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E),

(79)

where the last map
\kappa_{L}
sends
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\cdots,\psi_{k})
to
(\psi_{i}-\psi_{i+1})_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}
(see
19
). The composition of the morphism
\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}

(
resp., the map
\omega^{\kappa}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}

)
with the natural morphism
X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}

(
resp., the map
X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

)
of groupoids gives a morphism

\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}:X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
of groupoids over
{\rm Art}_{E}

(
resp., a map

\omega^{\kappa}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}:X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\rightarrow\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
)
. Note that
\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
(resp.,
\omega^{\kappa}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}
) factors through
|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|

(
resp.,
|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

)
.

Lemma 4.8
.

We have

(1)

|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
is a subfunctor of
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
.

(2)

F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
(see Section
2.3
) is a subfunctor of
|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
. Moreover, the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa,\;(\ref{kappakappaL})}
\textstyle{|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega^{\kappa}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}}
\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E).}

(80)

Beweis.

We first to show
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
that is a subfunctor
|X_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}}|
, i.e., the
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
deforming
\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}
on a deformation
\mathcal{M}_{A}
is unique. This follows from an argument analogous to
[
12
, Proposition 6.2.8]
and
[
1
, Proposition 2.3.6]
. The proof proceeds by induction on the length of
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
, we should show that
\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}
is an
\Omega
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}_{A}
, then
\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
is uniquely determined as a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{A}
,

{\mathcal{M}}_{A,2}/{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
is uniquely determined as a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{A}/{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
, and so on. Now suppose that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
is another
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
\mathcal{M}_{A}
deforming
\mathcal{M}_{1}
. Observe that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}_{A}/\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
) is a successive extension of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
(resp.,
\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}_{1}
). Applying
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.5 (1)]
to the case
1=i<j
, we deduce
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1},\mathcal{M}_{A}/\mathcal{M}_{A,1})=0
by an easy dévissage argument. Therefore, we see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}\subset\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
. Then we are done since
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{A,1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
have the same rank. This completes the proof of Part (1) by proceeding as we did for
\mathcal{M}_{A,1}
step by step. Similar to the proof of Part (1), we see that
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
is a subfunctor of
|X_{{\mathbf{D}}}|
. The
[
12
, Lemma 6.2.5 (1)]
has to be replaced by Lemma
2.7
. Since the morphism
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}\rightarrow|X_{{\mathbf{D}}}|
factors through
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}\rightarrow|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
, we deduce from Part (1) that
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}
is a subfunctor of
|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
.
∎

The non-critical property of
{\mathbf{D}}
implies the following isomorphism of functors.

Proposition 4.9
.

The morphism
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}\hookrightarrow|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|
of functors induces an isomorphism.

Beweis.

For any
A\in{\rm Art}_{E}
and
(A,{\mathbf{D}}_{A},j_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet})\in X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}(A)
, the
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
induces an increasing filtration
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{A}:=({\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{A}}{\mathbf{D}}_{A})_{1\leq i\leq k}:=(\mathcal{M}_{A,i}\cap{\mathbf{D}}_{A})_{1\leq i\leq k}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
by
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
. Therefore, we get that

{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{A}}{\mathbf{D}}_{A}\bigg[\frac{1}{t}\bigg]={\rm gr}_{i}\mathcal{M}_{A}\bigg[\frac{1}{t}\bigg]=\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta_{A,i})\bigg[\frac{1}{t}\bigg]

for any
1\leq i\leq k
. Note that
(\mathcal{M}_{A,i}\cap{\mathbf{D}}_{A})_{1\leq i\leq k}
may fail to be projective over
A
, and thus
\mathcal{F}_{A}
only gives an unsaturated filtration of
{\mathbf{D}}_{A}
. We first show that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{E}=\mathcal{F}_{E}
. By an easy variation of
[
1
, Lemma 2.4.2]
, we see that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{E}
is a saturated filtration of
{\mathbf{D}}
. Let
\{{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{\tau,1},{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{\tau,2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{\tau,ir}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}}
. By Lemma
2.7
, we see that
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}/{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}})=0
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}/{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}})=0
) for
1\leq i\leq k
. Therefore, we see that
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}}\subset{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subset{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}}
). For
1\leq i\leq k
, we further recall that
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}},{\rm Fil}_{i-1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}})=0
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}},{\rm Fil}_{i-1}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}})=0
). Therefore. We have two injections of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{1-k}){z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{(i-1)r+1}}})\hookrightarrow{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{1-k}){z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}}}),

(81)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{1-k}){z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{(i-1)r+1}}})\hookrightarrow{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{\pi}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}({\rm unr}(q_{L}^{1-k}){z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{ir}}}).

This implies that
{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{(i-1)r+1}\geq{{\mathbf{h}}}_{ir}
and
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{(i-1)r+1}\geq{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{ir}
. This implies
{{\mathbf{h}}}^{\prime}_{\tau,i}={{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}
for each
1\leq i\leq k
and
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
by the non-critical assumption. By the uniqueness of
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration with the parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
, we conclude that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{E}=\mathcal{F}_{E}
. Furthermore, by the above discussion and the uniqueness of
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration with the parameter
({\mathbf{x}}_{\pi},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}}
, we also see that the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{A}
on
{\mathbf{D}}_{A}
coincides with
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
when module
{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
. Similar to the proof of
[
1
, Lemma 2.2.3]
, we get the result.  ∎

We now prove the main results of this section, which assert that
X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)
(resp.,
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}

(resp.,

).

(resp.,

\displaystyle d\omega_{x_{L}}^{\Box,1}\;(\text{resp}.,\;d\omega_{x_{L}}^{\Box,2}):T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),X_{L}}\longrightarrow T_{{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}},\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi}}(\text{resp}.,\;T_{{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\underline{\mathbf{1}}}).

.

We identify

-valued point

\displaystyle d\omega_{x_{L}}^{\Box}:T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),X_{L}}\longrightarrow T_{{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\omega(x_{L})}

\displaystyle=T_{{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}},\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi}}\oplus T_{{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\underline{\mathbf{1}}}

\displaystyle\cong T_{{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}},\mathrm{\mathbf{q}}_{k}}\xrightarrow{-\cdot(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{ir}})_{1\leq i\leq k}}T_{{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}.\;

with

, we can get a map

\displaystyle\eta^{\Box}:\;T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),x_{L}}\xrightarrow{d\omega_{x_{L}}^{\Box}}T_{{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}

\displaystyle\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L^{\times}),E)=\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)

(86)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\kappa_{L}}\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{k}}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E).

Let
X_{\rho_{L}}
be the groupoid over

of deformations of the group morphism

. The map

(see (

)) induces a natural morphism

. By

, Lemma 4.13]

-vector spaces

Let

Proposition 4.10
.

is smooth at the point
x_{L}
, and (

) factors thought a surjective map

(88)

Beweis.

We first show that the image of
f_{\rho_{L}}
in (

) is contained in the

-vector space

|X_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}|(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\cong F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{M}_{A,\bullet}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{A})\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta_{A,i}\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]

(89)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pi_{0}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}\big({\rm unr}(a_{A,i}\alpha_{\pi}q_{L}^{i-k})\cdot({\delta}_{A,i})_{\varpi_{L}}\cdot z^{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{(i-1)r+1}}\big)\Big[\frac{1}{t}\Big]

The closed embedding

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow K(\rho_{L})\cap T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),X_{L}}\rightarrow T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),X_{L}}\xrightarrow{f_{\rho_{L}}}F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}).

. At last, by comparing the construction, we see that the composition

\displaystyle T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),X_{L}}\xrightarrow{f_{\rho_{L}}}F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}^{0}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})\xrightarrow{\omega_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}),(\ref{omegadeltapinon})}\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})=T_{{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L}},\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}

(91)

coincides with

(see (

)). We deduce from Corollary

the surjection (

).  ∎

We now prove the main proposition of this section. The natural embedding

{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}

induces the weight map

\omega:{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}\longrightarrow{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}.\;

(92)

Consider the tangent map of
\omega
at point
x=(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\rho_{L},\breve{\mathbf{{x}}}_{\pi},\underline{\mathbf{1}})
:

(93)

where the second isomorphism follows by the same argument of (

).

Proposition 4.11
.

{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}

, and (

) factors through a surjective map

Beweis.

Recall that
\mathcal{E}(x)
is the union of irreducible components of

. By (

) and (

), we see that

\displaystyle T_{{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})},x}\cong T_{\mathcal{E}(x),x}\cong

\displaystyle T_{X^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\times{\mathbb{U}}^{g}\times\iota_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}^{-1}(X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x)),(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},x_{L})}

(95)

\displaystyle\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow T_{X^{X^{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{aut}}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}(x),x_{L}}\xrightarrow{\eta^{\Box},\;(\ref{tangentLinveta})}\prod_{ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E).\;

Now the result follows.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

Let
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
be the groupoid over
{\rm Art}_{E}
defined in
[
12
, Section 6.4]
. For
w\in\mathscr{W}_{n,\Sigma_{L}}
, let
X^{w}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
be the closed subgroupoid of
X_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
defined in
[
12
, Section 6.4]
, which is closely related to some varieties studied in geometric representation theory. Since the parameters of our
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
are non-generic (in the sense of
[
12
, (6.5)]
), the morphism

X_{\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}\rightarrow\widehat{(\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},L})}_{\bm{\delta}_{{\mathbf{h}}}}\times_{\widehat{\mathfrak{z}}^{\langle r\rangle}_{L}}X_{W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}),\mathcal{F}},\;\mathcal{F}:=W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{\bullet})

of groupoids in
[
12
, Theorem 6.2.6]
is no longer formally smooth. Thus the discussions in
[
12
, Section 6.4]
cannot be applied to our case (for example, the final result
[
12
, Corollary 6.4.7]
). In our non-critical special case, it seems likely that
{\widehat{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\mathbf{{h}}}}(\overline{r})_{x_{L}}}
is also isomorphic to
X^{w_{0}}_{\rho_{L},\mathcal{M}_{\bullet}}
.

4.5
Local-global compatibility and the main theorem

We are ready to establish the second main theorem. Suppose that
\rho_{L}
appears in the patched Bernstein eigenvariety
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
, i.e. there exist
x^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\in({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})^{{\rm rig}}
, and

(\pi_{x,{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}},\chi)\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}

such that
x:=(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\rho_{L},\pi_{x,{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}},\chi)\in{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}
be the maximal ideal of
R_{\infty}[1/p]
corresponding to the point

of
({\mathrm{Spf}}\;R_{\infty})^{{\rm rig}}
(e.g. if
\rho_{L}
is attached to an automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}
with non-zero

-fixed vectors). By the argument in Section
4.1
(especially, the continuous
R_{\infty}
-admissible unitary representation
\Pi_{\infty}
of
G
over
E
) or

[
21
]
, we get that

is an admissible unitary Banach representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
, which one might expect to be the right representation (up to multiplicities) corresponding to
\rho_{L}
in the
p
-adic local Langlands program. Suppose the following holds:

(a)

The
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}=D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
admits a non-critical special
\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration with parameter
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{\pi,{\mathbf{h}}},\widetilde{\bm{\delta}}_{\mathbf{h}})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
(see Definition
2.4
);

(b)

\rho_{L}
is potentially semi-stable;

(c)

The monodromy operator
N
on

satisfies

.

By the argument before
[
12
, Corollary 3.1.11]
,
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
has an action of
\mu_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
. For

, we have

{\psi}\cdot((x_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k},(\chi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k})=((x_{i}{\otimes_{E}}{\rm unr}(\psi_{i}(\varpi_{L})))_{1\leq i\leq k},(\chi_{i}\psi_{i}|_{\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}})_{1\leq i\leq k}),\;{\psi}=(\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq k}\in\mu_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}.

By Proposition
4.1
(1),
(x^{{\mathfrak{p}}},\rho_{L},\pi_{x,{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}},\chi)
appears in patched Berstein eigenvariety

if and only if

. By Proposition

(4) (where we use the theory of

\Omega_{[1,k]}
-filtration with the parameter

and proof of Proposition

, we deduce

Lemma 4.13
.

-orbit of the point

\displaystyle\cong

Proposition 4.14
.

The natural map constructed in (

)

(100)

Beweis.

The proof is divided into the following steps.

(a)
Let
f\in{\rm Hom}_{G}\Big({\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\Big)

factors through

W^{\prime}
is an irreducible constituent of
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
for some

. If

is locally algebraic (i.e.,

), then

is an irreducible constituent of

. But

, Theorem]

,

\displaystyle\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\Big(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}{\otimes_{E}}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),J_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\big)\Big),

102
) via
R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]\twoheadrightarrow R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]/{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}\cong E
to make that the
(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}},{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))
-equivariant morphism
f
is also
R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
-equivariant. Let
V^{\prime}
denote the pushfoward of
M^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))
via
f
. Then we get an exact sequence of
(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}},{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))
-modules (endowed with natural continuous
R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
actions)

0\rightarrow V\rightarrow V^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\beta_{0}}L(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))\rightarrow 0.\;

(103)

It suffices to construct a section of
\beta_{0}
(as
(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}},{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))\times R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
-equivariant morphism. Indeed, if we can obtain a section
s_{0}
of
\beta_{0}
, then
s_{0}\circ f^{\prime}
gives a desired lifting of
f
to
M^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))
, and gives an
(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}},{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))
-morphism
M^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))\rightarrow V[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
.
Pulling back the exact sequence (
103
) along the quotient map
M^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow L(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
, we get an exact sequence

0\rightarrow V\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\beta_{1}}M^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))\rightarrow 0.\;

(104)

By Lemma
4.13
and the argument after
[
26
, (80)]
or
[
3
, (15)]
, it suffices to construct a
(\mathfrak{g}_{\Sigma_{L}},{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L))\times R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
-equivariant map

M^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime}.

(105)

It remains to construct a
{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)\times R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
-equivariant map

L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha))\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime}.

(106)

By using Proposition
4.16
below and taking the
{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}
-generalized eigenspaces for
R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
, we can get an
R_{\infty}[\frac{1}{p}]
-equivariant exact sequence of finite-dimensional
E
-vector spaces

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\rightarrow B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\lambda_{\mathbf{h}}}(V)[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=d\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty},{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}}]

(107)

\displaystyle\rightarrow B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\lambda_{\mathbf{h}}}(V^{\prime\prime})[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=d\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty},{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\lambda_{\mathbf{h}}}(L(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha)))[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=d\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty},{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0.

where
\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}
denotes the central character of
\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha){\otimes_{E}}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
, and
{\mathfrak{m}}
denotes the maximal ideal of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
associated to
\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}
. By Lemma
4.13
, we deduce that

B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\lambda_{\mathbf{h}}}(V)[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=d\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty},{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}}]=0.

Hence we obtain an isomorphism

\displaystyle B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\lambda_{\mathbf{h}}}(V^{\prime\prime})[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=d\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty},{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}}]

(108)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},\lambda_{\mathbf{h}}}(L(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha)))[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=d\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty},{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\omega_{s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}}]

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}{\otimes_{E}}L^{{\langle r\rangle}}(s\cdot{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),

where the second isomorphism follows from
[
27
, Lemma 3.5.2]
. Then (

) is induced by the inverse of (

) and
[
27
, Theorem 3.5.6]
.
∎

The proof of
[
3
, Example 4.2]
gives the following Lemma.

Lemma 4.15
.

Suppose that
V
is an admissible continuous Banach representation of
G
, and there exists a compact open subgroup
H
such that
V|_{H}\xrightarrow{\sim}C^{0}(H,L)^{\oplus l}
. Then

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}\big(\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Sigma_{L}},V^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\otimes U^{\vee}\big)=0,

(109)

for all
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and any finite-dimensional
E
-linear locally analytic representation
U
of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
.

The following proposition generalizes a result of
[
19
, Proposition 4.1]
. Let
{\mathfrak{o}}
be an integral weight of
{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})_{{\mathfrak{o}}}
be the fibre of
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
at
{\mathfrak{o}}
via the morphism

{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}\rightarrow({\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{\vee},\;

where
{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}\rightarrow({\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})^{\vee}
is the differentiation map
\chi\mapsto d\chi
. We use
\iota_{0}({\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})
to emphasize the action on
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})
derived from
\iota_{0}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
. For an
E
-algebra
A
,
{\mathfrak{m}}\subset A
an ideal, and an
A
-module, we denote by
M[{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}]
the
A
-submodule of
M
consisting of elements annihilated by
{\mathfrak{m}}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. Then
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.4]
shows that

\displaystyle B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[\iota_{0}({\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})={\mathfrak{o}}]

(110)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\delta,\chi}B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[\iota_{0}({\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})={\mathfrak{o}}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta}^{\infty}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{{\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm MaxSpec}\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}},\chi}B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[\iota_{0}({\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}})={\mathfrak{o}}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}],\;

where
\delta
(resp.
\chi
) runs through the smooth characters of
\Delta_{0}
(see
[
12
, (3.4)]
) (resp. through the locally algebraic character of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
of weight
{\mathfrak{o}}
), and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta}\subset E[\Delta_{0}]
(resp.
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}\subset E[{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})]
) is the maximal ideal associated to
\delta
(resp.
\chi
). Replacing character
\delta
of
\Delta_{0}
by maximal ideals
{\mathfrak{m}}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
, we can get the second identity by the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.4]
. By
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.4]
, we see that each term in the direct sums is finite-dimensional over
E
.

Proposition 4.16
.

Suppose that
V
is an admissible continuous Banach representation of
G
and there exists a compact open subgroup
H
such that
V|_{H}\xrightarrow{\sim}C^{0}(H,L)^{\oplus l}
. Assume that

0\rightarrow V^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}\rightarrow\Pi\rightarrow\Pi_{1}\rightarrow 0,

(111)

is an exact sequence of admissible locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
G
. Let
\chi:{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})\rightarrow E^{\times}
be a continuous character of weight
{\mathfrak{d}}
, and let
{\mathfrak{m}}
be a maximal ideal of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
. Now we have short exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow(V^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}})^{{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)_{0}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}},{\mathfrak{d}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=0]\rightarrow

\displaystyle\Pi^{{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)_{0}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}},{\mathfrak{d}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=0]

(112)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Pi_{1}^{{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)_{0}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}},{\mathfrak{d}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}=0]\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(V^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}})[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}]\rightarrow

\displaystyle B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi)[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}]

(113)

\displaystyle\rightarrow B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{1})[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}][{\mathfrak{m}}^{\infty}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}]\rightarrow 0.

Moreover, all the vector spaces in the last exact sequence are finite-dimensional.

Beweis.

The above proposition implies that taking
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\Sigma_{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}
-invariant is exact on the short exact sequence (
111
). Since taking
{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)_{0}
-invariant is exact on the category of smooth representations of
{\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)_{0}
, we deduce the first exact sequence (
112
). To see the exactness of the second sequence of (
113
), we need to unwind the action of
\Delta_{0}
,
\iota_{0}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
and
\iota_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
on
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(W)
for
W\in\{V^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}},\Pi,\Pi_{1}\}
. As in the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.4]
, we can choose a compact open subgroup of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
such that
B_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(W)[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}]
is a
\Delta_{0}
-equivalent direct summand of

\displaystyle\big(J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}

\displaystyle(W)_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}\circ_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}]\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}]\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\vee}\big)^{H}

\displaystyle\cong\;\bigoplus_{\delta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\big(\big(J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(W)_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\vee}\big)^{H^{D}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{\infty}]

\displaystyle\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}]\big)^{Z_{H}},

where
H^{D}:=H\cap{\mathbf{D}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
(resp.,
Z_{H}:=H\cap{\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
), and
\delta^{\prime}
runs though the locally algebraic characters of
\iota_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
(and
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{\infty}]
is for the corresponding
\iota_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
-action). Similar to the proof of
[
19
, Proposition 4.1, (4.3), Pages 10521-10522]
, we have a short exact sequence of finite-dimensional
E
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\big(J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(V^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}

\displaystyle\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\vee}\big)^{H^{D}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}\circ_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{\infty}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\big(J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi)_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\vee}\big)^{H^{D}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}\circ_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{\infty}]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\big(J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{1})_{{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\vee}\big)^{H^{D}}[{\mathfrak{z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}={\mathfrak{o}}\circ_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta^{\prime}}^{\infty}]\rightarrow 0.\;

Then the result follows by taking
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\chi}]
-eigenspaces and replacing character
\delta^{\prime}
of
\Delta_{0}
by maximal ideals
{\mathfrak{m}}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{\Omega_{[1,k]}}
, as in the proof of
[
12
, Lemma 3.1.4]
.
∎

Let
{\mathfrak{I}}_{y}\subseteq{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}
be a closed ideal of
R_{\infty}[1/p]
such that
\dim_{E}(R_{\infty}[1/p]/{\mathfrak{I}}_{y})<+\infty
and that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}
is the unique closed maximal ideal containing
{\mathfrak{I}}_{y}
(e.g.
{\mathfrak{I}}_{y}={\mathfrak{m}}_{y}^{k}
). Similar to
[
25
, Corollary 4.9]
, we can obtain

Corollary 4.17
.

Let
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\subseteq W\subseteq{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
. If a morphism

f:W{\longrightarrow}\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{y}]

satisfies that
f|_{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})}\subseteq\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
, then
f
has image in
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
.

The following theorem is the main result of this paper. As soon as we finish the previous preparation, the proof is almost completely parallel to the proof of
[
26
, Theorem 4.10]
. We include a proof for the reader’s convenience.

Theorem 4.18
.

(1) The following restriction map is bijective

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{G}\big(\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})),

\displaystyle\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\big)

(114)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{G}\big({\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\big).

(2) Let
0\neq\psi\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
and
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
, an injection

f:{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]

can extend to an injection
\Sigma_{i}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
if and only if
\psi\in\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir}
.

The rest of the section is to prove Theorem
4.18
, and we use the strategy of (
[
26
]
).

Beweis.

(a)
The “if " part of Theorem
4.18
(2) is a consequence of (1). It suffices to prove the “only if " part. Otherwise, we assume that there exists
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
with
\psi\notin\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir}
. Recall that
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir}
is of codimension
1
in
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, we see that
\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir}+E\psi={\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
. Then the injection

\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir})\oplus_{\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]

induces

\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi_{\infty})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir})\oplus_{\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}].

for any
0\neq\psi_{\infty}\in{\rm Hom}_{\infty}(L^{\times},E)
. But by
[
34
, Remark 5.21]
,
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi_{\infty})
contains
V\otimes_{E}L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
where
V
is a smooth extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{\infty}(\pi)
by
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\pi)
. By applying the (left exact) Jacquet-Emerton functor to
V\otimes_{E}L({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
, we get a contradiction with Lemma
4.13
. This completes the proof of (2).

(b)
Since
{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}={\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
, it follows from Lemma
4.13
and the step (a) of the proof of Proposition
4.14
) that the injectivity of (
114
) holds. It remains to show that (
114
) is surjective. By definition, it suffices to show that for any
ir\in\Delta_{n}(k)
,
\psi\in\mathcal{L}(\rho_{L})_{ir}
, the following restriction map is surjective

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm GL}_{n}(L)}\big(\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\big)\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm GL}_{n}(L)}\big({\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\big).

(115)

The key ingredient is the consequence of the surjectivity of (
94
).
By Corollary
4.11
, there exists a
t:\rm SpecE[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
(as an element in
T_{{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})},x}
) such that the
i
-th factor of the image of
t
under (
94
) equals
\psi
, and the
j
-th factors for all
j\neq i
are zero. Let
\Psi\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),E)
be the image of
t
via the first map in (
95
). By Proposition
4.1
(3), we see that the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}
is Cohen-Macaulay over
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
. Since
non-critical special point
x
is a smooth point on
{\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\mathfrak{p}},\bm{\lambda}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})}
, we see that
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}
is locally free in a certain neighborhood of
x
. let
{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}
denotes the kernel of the morphism

induced by
t
. We put

\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi):=\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}){\otimes_{E}}({1+\Psi\epsilon})\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}.\;

By the construction of
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}
, we deduce the following facts:

(a)

(x^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}},{J}_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\big)\big)
, and

(t^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}},J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}]\big)\big)
,

which are closed subrepresentations of
J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}\big(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}])
and
J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}])
respectively;

(b)

there are natural
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
-equivariant injections

(x^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}})^{\vee}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow(t^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}})^{\vee}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}]).

(116)

By Lemma
4.13
and an easy variation of the proof of

[
26
, Lemma 4.11]
, we can show that

Lemma 4.19
.

The morphisms of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
-representations:

\displaystyle(x^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\infty,\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}})^{\vee}\hookrightarrow J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]),

(117)

\displaystyle\text{and\;}

\displaystyle(t^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\infty,\Omega_{[1,k]},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}})^{\vee}\hookrightarrow J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}])

are balanced (see
[
29
, Definition 0.8]
,
[
30
, Definition 5.17]
).

Beweis.

This lemma follows by an easy variation of the proof of
[
26
, Lemma 4.11]
. We briefly indicate below the changes. The notation ”
U({\mathbf{B}}(L))
” (resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\rm sm}}_{c}\big({\mathbf{N}}(L),-)
, resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\rm lp}}_{c}\big({\mathbf{N}}(L),-)
, resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm pol}}_{c}\big({\mathbf{N}}(L),-)
) has to be replaced by
\text{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{\Sigma_{L}})
(resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\rm sm}}_{c}\big({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),-)
, resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\rm lp}}_{c}\big({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),-)
, resp.,
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm pol}}_{c}\big({\mathbf{N}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L),-)
). The short exact sequence “
0\rightarrow\chi\rightarrow\widetilde{\chi}\rightarrow\chi\rightarrow 0
" has to be replaced by the short exact sequence

``0{\longrightarrow}\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}){\otimes_{E}}\delta_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}{\longrightarrow}0".\;

The "
[
26
, Lemma 4.6]
" has to be replaced by Lemmas
4.13
.
∎

This lemma shows that the adjunction formula in
[
29
]
is suitable for us. Let
V^{\prime\prime}
be a locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)
. We refer to
[
29
]
for the detail of locally
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation
I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(V^{\prime\prime})
of
G
, which is a closed
G
-subrepresentation of
({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}V^{\prime\prime})^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
. By
[
29
, Theorem 0.13]
, there exists an integer
r
, such that the injections in (
116
) induce

I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}\big(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\big)^{\oplus r}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}\big(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi)\big)^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}].

(118)

By
[
29
, Proposition 2.8.10]
, we have
I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))\cong i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
. The natural exact sequence

0{\longrightarrow}\;\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\;{\longrightarrow}\;\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi)\;{\longrightarrow}\;\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\;{\longrightarrow}0

induces a sequence (not necessary exact)

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}).

Then
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
is an irreducible constituent in
I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))
of multiplicity
2
.

Using the natural embedding

I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))\hookrightarrow\Big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi)\Big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}},\;

we can take inside
\Big({\rm Ind}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}^{G}\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi)\Big)^{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
the following intersections:

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))\cap\Big(\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\Big),

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))\cap{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}).

Thus
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}:=W/U
is a subrepresentation of
{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
, and
I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))/U
is an extension of
i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
by
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}
. As in Step (a) of the proof of Proposition
4.14
, we deduce from Lemma
4.13
that any irreducible constituent of
U
can not appear in the socle of
\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}]
. Then (
118
) induces

{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\oplus r}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\big(I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))/U\big)^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}].

(119)

This composition is injective. Since
(x^{*}\mathcal{M}_{\infty})^{\vee}
has image in
J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(L)}(\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}])
via (
116
), we see that it factors through an injection
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}]
by Corollary
4.17
.

Denote by
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}\cap\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
. Then
[
34
, Proposition 5.28]
deduce
{\rm soc}_{G}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}\cong{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
. Thus by Corollary
4.17
, the composition

(\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime})^{\oplus r}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\big(I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))/U\big)^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}]

(120)

also has image in
\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
. By Lemma
4.13
, it is also injective. By Proposition
4.14
, we see that (
120
) extends uniquely to an injection

\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}].

(121)

Combining (
120
) with (
121
), we put

V^{+}:=\big(I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))/U\big)\oplus_{\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}}\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}).

Therefore, (
120
) and (
121
) give

\displaystyle\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\oplus r}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\big(V^{+}\big)^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}].

(122)

It has image in
\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
. It suffices to prove the following assertion.

Claim.
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)
is a subrepresentation of
V^{+}
.

Proof of the claim.
Let
V
be the pull-back of
I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))/U
via the injection

i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}).\;

Then
V
lies in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\textstyle{\hskip 20.0ptV\hskip 20.0pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\subseteq}
\textstyle{i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}_{\{i\}}^{\emptyset}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi)^{0}/\widetilde{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

(123)

where
\widetilde{U}:=\sum_{\emptyset\neq I\subseteq\Delta_{n}(k)}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
. Recall that the representation

\mathscr{E}_{\{ir\}}^{\emptyset}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi)^{0}
is defined in the argument below
[
34
, Theorem 5.19]
. By
[
34
, Theorem 5.19]
and
[
34
, Remark 5.21]
, the pull-back of the bottom exact sequence via the injection

w_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}):=w_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})\otimes_{E}{\rm unr}(\alpha)\circ{\rm det}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow i_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{G}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})

(124)

is split. Since
{\rm Hom}_{G}(w_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{\infty}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})/\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime})=0
, we deduce

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(w_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(w_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),{\rm St}_{(r,k)}^{{\rm an}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}))

Therefore, the pull-back of the top exact sequence of (
123
) via (
124
) is also split. This implies that

I_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}}^{G}(\pi_{0}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\Psi))/U
contains a subrepresentation
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)^{\prime}
, which is isomorphic to an extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
by
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}
. We have an isomorphism

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{I}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0}.{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),\widetilde{\Sigma}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}),

by similar strategy in the proof of
[
34
, Proposition 5.29]
and
[
34
, Proposition 5.34(2)]
. This asserts that

\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)^{\prime}
comes from some
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)^{\prime}
, which is
an extension of
v_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}^{{\langle r\rangle}}_{ir}}^{\infty}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
by the representation
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}
. But the push-forward of
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)^{\prime}
via the injection
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})
is isomorphic to
\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)
. The claim follows.

The composition in (
122
) induces then

\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}})^{\oplus r}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Sigma_{i}^{{\langle r\rangle}}(\alpha,\pi_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}},\psi)^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{I}}_{t}].

By using the same argument as in the
[
25
, Page. 8040]
, we see that the image of the second morphism is also contained in
\pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}]
. The surjectivity of (
115
) now follows. We complete the proof of Theorem
4.18
.
∎
